SEC. G. C. 

RELIEF, HOW OBTAINED; NOTICE TO TRUSTEES OR OFFICERS. 

When a person in a township or municipal corporation requires public relief, or the services of a physician or surgeon, complaint thereof shall be forthwith made by a person having knowledge of the fact to the township trustees, or proper municipal officer. If medical services are required, and no physician or surgeon is regularly employed by contract to furnish medical attendance to such poor, the physician called or attending shall immediately notify such trustees or officer, in writing, that he is attending such person, and thereupon the township or municipal corporation shall be liable for relief and services thereafter rendered such person. If such services consist of hospital care rendered such person, such hospital shall be paid such amount as may be agreed upon by such trustees or proper officers and such hospital, or if no such agreement is made then such hospital shall be paid the established ward rate for such care in such hospital. If such notice be not given within three days after such relief is afforded or services begin, the township or municipal corporation shall be liable only for relief or services rendered after notice has been given. Such trustees or officer, at any time may order the discontinuance of such services, and shall not be liable for services or relief thereafter rendered.7 

DISCUSSION: 

See Sec. 3391 to 3391-12 inclusive of the General Code which modifies this section. 

A physician who has complied with the notice required by this section has a claim against a local relief area for services rendered an indigent for no greater amount than the proper officers determine to be just and reasonable, and where they have considered a claim and have allowed the same in part or rejected it, in good faith, no action can be maintained thereon.8 

Notice is a condition precedent to recovery for medical services.9 

Medical services include hospitalization, 1 chiropractic service2 and surgical service.3

If a local relief area has employed a physician, no recovery can be made by another physician.4

SEC. 3480-1 G. C. PROCEEDINGS WHEN INDIGENT PER- SON IS FURNISHED WITH MEDICAL SERVICE BY TOWNSHIP OR CITY OF WHICH HE IS A NON-RESI- DENT, BUT IN THE SAME COUNTY IN WHICH SUCH PERSON HAS A LEGAL SETTLEMENT. 

When an indigent person requiring medical services or the services of a hospital, in cases other than contagious, has a legal settlement in a municipality or township within the same county but other than that in which the service is rendered, and such person is unable to pay the expenses of such service, the county, municipality or township rendering such service shall notify, in writing, the proper officials of the municipality or township of legal settlement of such person that services are being rendered. Such written notice shall be sent within three days if the fact of non-residence is disclosed upon the beginning of such service or admission to such hospital, or within three days after discovery of such fact if the same be not disclosed as above. Thereupon the municipality or township of legal settlement shall be liable for such services at the established rate of the county, municipality or township rendering such service and shall pay for the same within thirty days after date of the sworn statement covering such expenses, which sworn statement shall be sent to the proper officials of the municipality or township of legal settlement within twenty days after the 

5. 1923 Opin. Atty. Gen., p. 557. 

6. 1928 Opin. Atty. Gen., No. 1519; 1939 Opin. Atty. Gen., No. 135. 

1928 Opin. Atty. Gen., No. 1903. 

238

discharge of such person. If the notice of such service be not sent to the municipality or township of legal settlement within three days after the disclosure by such person or the discovery of such non-residence, such municipality or township shall be liable only after the receipt of such notice. 

Nothing herein contained shall prevent the removal of such person, or the assumption of care of such person, by the municipality or township of legal settlement, at its expense, but such removal or assumption shall not relieve such municipality or township from liability for the expenses theretofore incurred by the county, municipality or township rendering such service. The municipality or township of legal settlement is hereby subrogated to all rights of the county, municipality or township rendering such service to such person.8

DISCUSSION: 

Hospital services must still be furnished by township trustees to indigent resident of the township.9 

This section applies only to medical and hospital services, and trustees or city officials cannot recover for general relief furnished non-resident of such township or city.10

When a person resident of the county but a non-resident of the township or city in which he is temporarily residing is in need of medical or hospital services, the township or city in which found should pay for such services, and charge the same to the township or city of legal residence.1

When complaint is made to the township trustees or to the proper officers of the municipal corporation that a person therein requires public relief or support, one or more of such officers, or some other duly authorized person, shall visit the person needing relief, forthwith, to ascertain his name, age, 8. sex, color, nativity, length of residence in the county, previous habits and present condition and in what township and county in this state he is legally settled. The information so ascertained shall be transmitted to the township clerk, or proper officer of the municipal corporation, and recorded on the proper records. No relief or support shall be given to a person without such visitation or investigation, except that within counties, where there is maintained a public charity organization, or other benevolent association, which investigates and keeps a record of facts relating to persons who receive or apply for relief, the infirmary superintendents, township trustees or officers of a city shall accept such investigation and information and may grant relief upon the approval and recommendation of such organization. Every reasonable effort shall be made by the township trustees and municipal officers to secure aid from relatives and interested organizations before granting relief from public funds.2

DISCUSSION: 

While this section authorizes an investigation by some one duly authorized by the township trustees or city officials to investigate applications for relief, no authority exists for township trustees to employ some person either to make investigations or keep records in connection therewith and pay compensation therefor.3 

SEC. 3482 G. C. REMOVAL OF FOREIGN PAUPERS TO THEIR OWN COUNTY. 

When it has been so ascertained that a person requiring relief has a legal settlement in some other county of the state, such trustees or officers shall immediately notify the infirmary superintendent of the county in which the person is found, who, if his health permits, shall immediately remove the person to the infirmary of the county of his legal settlement. If such person refuses to be removed, on the complaint being made by the infirmary superintendent, the probate judge of the county in which the person is found shall issue a warrant for such removal, and the county wherein the legal settlement of the person is, shall pay all expenses of such removal and the necessary charges for relief and in case of death the expense of burial if a written notice is given the county commissioners thereof within twenty days after such legal settlement has been ascertained.4

DISCUSSION: 

This section authorizes direct means for the removal of paupers having a legal settlement in some other county of the state.5

Upon refusal or failure to pay such expenses, such board of county commissioners may be compelled so to do by a civil action against them by the board of county commissioners of the county from which such person is removed, in the court of common pleas of the county to which such removal is made.

SEC. 3484 G. C. REMOVAL OF INDIGENT PERSONS TO THEIR OWN COUNTIES. 

When the trustees of a township, or proper officers of a municipal corporation in a county in the state in which there is no county infirmary ascertain that any person, requiring relief, in such township or municipal corporation has a legal settlement in another county of the state, they shall immediately notify the board of county commissioners thereof to remove such person to the infirmary of such county. Should his health permit, such board of county commissioners shall immediately remove such person to their infirmary, and, if within twenty days after such legal settlement is as ertained, a written notice is given them, to pay all expenses theretofore incurred for his relief in the township or municipal corporation in which such person is found. Upon their refusal or failure to so remove such person, the trustees of such township, or proper officers of a municipal corporation, may furnish him the necessary relief and collect the amount thereof from such board of county commissioners by a civil action, in the name of such township trustees, or of such municipal officers in the court of common pleas of the county in which such infirmary is situated.8

DISCUSSION: 

Non-residents in need of public relief other than medical or surgical, or the services of a hospital should be removed to the county of legal settlement if their health permits. If unable to be removed, aid may be extended to them in their homes by the local relief authority of the county of legal settlement.9

SEC. 3484-1 G. C. ACTION WHEN INDIGENT PERSONS REFUSE TO BE REMOVED TO THEIR OWN COUN- 

TIES.

If a person requiring relief whose legal settlement has been ascertained to be in some other county of the state refuses to be removed thereto, pursuant to G. C. Sec. 3482 or G. C. 

Sec. 3484, on complaint being made by the officer whose duty it is to remove him, the probate judge of the county in which the person is found shall issue a warrant for such removal. 

In addition to all other proceedings for the removal of a person requiring relief to another county of the state wherein his legal settlement may be, the township trustees or the proper officers of the municipal corporation in which a person requiring public relief is found or resident taxpayer of the county may institute proceedings in the probate court of such county to determine the legal settlement of such person and procure his removal thereto. Such proceedings shall be by petition which shall be sufficient if it states the facts required by G. C. Sec. 3481 to be ascertained. The county commissioners of the county in which such person is alleged to have a legal settlement shall be made parties and summons issued to them as in civil actions. The proceedings may be set down for hearing at any time after the return day of the summons and shall be deemed at issue without further pleading. If upon the evidence the person is found to require public relief or support and that he is legally settled in the township and county alleged in the petition a warrant for his removal to said county shall be issued by the probate judge and judgment shall be rendered for costs and all charges and expenditures for which the commissioners of said county shall be liable by virtue of notice similar to that provided for in 

G. C. Secs. 3482 and 3483 which notice for the purpose of action herein provided for may be given by a board, officer or person authorized to bring such action.10

DISCUSSION: 

It has been held that such action should be filed in the county where the legal settlement is alleged to be,¹ although the Supreme Court has indicated in another case that the action should be filed in the county where the indigent is found.2 10.

SEC. 3484-2 G. C. PROCEEDINGS WHEN INDIGENT PER- SON FURNISHED WITH MEDICAL SERVICES IN COUNTY OTHER THAN COUNTY OF LEGAL SET- TLEMENT. 

When a person requiring medical services or the services of a hospital, in cases other than contagious, has a legal settlement in a county other than the one in which such service is rendered, and is unable to pay the expenses of such service, and such service is rendered by a county, municipality or township, the county, municipality or township rendering such service shall notify in writing the county commissioners of the county of legal settlement that such service is being rendered. Such written notice shall be sent within three days. if the fact of non-residence is disclosed upon the beginning of such service, or admission to such hospital, or within three days after the discovery of such fact, if the same be not disclosed as above. Within twenty days after the discharge of such person, or the rendering of the last service, the county, municipality or township rendering such service shall send a notice thereof, and a sworn statement of its expenses, at the established rate of the county, municipality or township therefor, to the county commissioners of the county of legal settlement. Thereupon the county of legal settlement shall be liable to the county, municipality or township rendering such service for the expenses of such service, including hospital service, at the established rate of the county, municipality or township therefor, and shall pay for the same within thirty days after date of the sworn statement of expenses. If the notice of the rendering of such service, required to be sent by the county, municipality or township rendering the same, be not sent within three days after the disclosure by such person, or the discovery of such non-residence, the county of legal settlement shall be liable only after the receipt of such notice. Nothing herein contained shall prevent the removal or assumption of care of such person by the county of legal settlement, at its expense, but such removal or assumption shall theret render discha last s lished deeme ship D settle count such tee leg со se th St st tl To

in - ser of wh G. NO To of fc 244

shall not relieve such county of liability for the expenses theretofore incurred by the county, municipality or township rendering such service. Any such person who does not, upon discharge from such hospital, or upon the rendering of the last service, pay the expenses of such service, at the established rate therefor, shall for the purpose of this act, be deemed indigent insofar as the county, municipality or township rendering such service is concerned. The county of legal settlement is hereby subrogated to all the rights of the county, municipality or township rendering such service to such person.3

DISCUSSION 

If an indigent person is hospitalized by township trustees or city officials in a county other than a county of legal settlement, and if proper notification is given to the county commissioners of the county of his legal settlement, the county commissioners of the county of legal settlement should pay the cost of such hospitalization at the established rate of the township or city providing such hospitalization, within thirty days after the sworn statement of expenses is received.4

Failure to give notice makes the county of legal settlement liable only after receipt of notice.5

The fact that such services were furnished by a doctor or hospital located in another state does not bar recovery.6

This section does not authorize a county maintaining a hospital to charge a foreign county for hospital services rendered persons legally resident of such foreign county in the absence of a contract.7

SEC. 3485 G. C. ACCOUNTS TO BE KEPT BY TRUSTEES AND CORPORATION OFFICERS; SETTLEMENTS.

The township trustees or proper officers of a municipal corporation shall keep accurate accounts of expenses so incurred, and make entries in a book of the names of the persons, and the time when each become chargeable, with an account of their own services rendered. Such account shall be adjusted and settled on the first Mondays of March and 

September. The clerk of the township or municipality shall record the accounts in the proper records, and issue orders on the treasurer thereof for the amount thus paid and services rendered as soon as paid or rendered. Such trustees or proper officer shall issue orders on the treasurer thereof for such demands when they accrue.8

SEC. 3486 G. C. 

ANNUAL REPORT TO AUDITOR. 

Immediately after the September settlement the township clerk or proper officer of a municipal corporation shall make and file with the county auditor a report of the administration of the poor laws in the township or municipality for the preceding year, showing all expenditures in that behalf as follows: First, the aggregate of physicians fees paid; second, the aggregate paid for supplies, food, clothing, etc.; third, aggregate of per diem and expenses of such trustees or municipal officers in connection with the poor laws.9

ACCOUNTS TO BE CERTIFIED. 

No account shall be audited or allowed by the trustees of a township or officers of a municipal corporation for the support of the poor, unless accompanied by the proper voucher, verified trust

SEC. 3488 G. C. RELIEF IN COUNTIES HAVING NO IN- FIRMARY. 

When the trustees of a township in a county having no county infirmary, are satisfied that a person in such township ought to have public relief they shall afford such relief at the expense of their township as in their opinion the necessities. of the person require. When more than temporary relief is required, they shall post a notice in three public places in the township, specifying a time and place at which they will receive proposals for the maintenance of such person, which notice shall be posted at least seven days before the day therein named for receiving proposals.1

SEC. 3489 G. C. CONTRACT FOR SUPPORT OF PERSON. 

The township trustees may contract with such person as they think suitable to take charge of and maintain such person, which shall be on the most reasonable terms, but not for more than one year at any one time. If the legal settlement of such person is not within this state, or is unknown, they shall keep an accurate account of moneys so expended, and certify it, with the vouchers therefor, to the county commissioners, who shall cause the amounts so paid to be refunded to such township from the county treasury, on the warrant of the county auditor.2

SEC. 3490 G. C. MEDICAL RELIEF OF POOR IN TOWN- SHIPS OR CORPORATIONS. 

The trustees of a township, or the proper officers of a municipal corporation in any county, may contract with one. or more competent physicians to furnish medical relief and medicines necessary for the persons who come under their charge under the poor laws, but no contract shall extend beyond one year. Such physician shall report quarterly to the clerk of the township or municipality, on blanks furnished him for that purpose, the names of all persons to whom he has furnished medical relief or medicines, the number of visits made in attending such person, the character of the disease, and such other information as may be required by such trustees or officers.3

SEC. 3491 G. C. TO WHOM CONTRACT SHALL BE 

AWARDED. 

Such contracts shall be given to the lowest competent bidder. The trustees or municipal officers shall reserve the right to reject any and all bids, and to annul such contract at any time for proper cause.4

SEC. 3492 G. C. 

NON-LIABILITY AFTER CONTRACT. 

When the township trustees or the officers of a municipal corporation enter into such contract, such township or municipality shall not be liable for any relief thereafter otherwise furnished such person, so long as such contract remains in force.5

SEC. 3493 G. C. 

PERFORMANCE OF LABOR BY RECIPI- ENT OF PUBLIC RELIEF. 

When public relief, not in a county or city infirmary is applied for, or afforded by the infirmary officials of any county or the trustees of a township or officers of a municipal corporation, and the applicant or recipient is able to do manual labor, such officers, shall require a male applicant or recipient to perform labor to the value of the relief afforded, at any time, upon any free public park, public highway, or other public property or public contract therein, under the direction of the proper authorities having charge or control thereof. If relief has been afforded and such recipient refuses to perform the labor provided, record of the fact shall be made, all relief or support thereafter refused him, and he may be proceeded against as a vagrant.6 

DISCUSSION: 

When an applicant in need of relief is given public employment which is paid in cash he is an employee under the workmen's compensation law.7 Those who are paid in goods are also employees.8 

SEC. 3494 G. C. CONTROL OF PAUPER'S PROPERTY BY COMMISSIONERS AND TRUSTEES. 

4.

Sec. 3491, G. C., R. S. 1499-2. 

5. Sec. 3492, G. C., 108 O. L., Pt. 1, 274. 6. 

Sec. 3493, G. C., 108 O. L., Pt. 1, 274. 

7. 1932 Opin. Atty. Gen., No. 4526. 8. 

Sec. 3496-1, et seq., G. C. 249

POOR RELIEF 

The commissioners of counties having no infirmary, the trustees of a township or the proper officer of a municipal corporation therein, shall have and may exercise the same rights. powers, and duties with reference to the property of persons coming under their charge under the poor laws of the state, as are conferred upon and exercised by infirmary officials in counties having infirmaries.9 

DISCUSSION: 

Sec. 2548, 2549 and 2550 G. C. authorizes the county commissioners or the officers of a county infirmary to petition the probate court in order to sell any real or personal property belonging to a person who is a county or city charge. The same proceedings are had as in the sale of real estate by guardian. The net proceeds of the sale are kept in a separate fund against which the cost of maintenance of such person is charged. If any amount remains after the death of such person, it is distributed to the legal representatives of such decedent.

SEC. 3495 G. C. BURIAL OF DEAD IN CERTAIN CASES. 

When the dead body of a person is found in a township or municipal corporation, and such person was not an inmate of a penal, reformatory, benevolent or charitable institution, in this state, and whose body is not claimed by any person for private interment at his own expense, or delivered for the purpose of medical or surgical study or dissection in accordance with the provisions of section 9984, it shall be disposed of as follows: If he were a legal resident of the county, the proper officers of the township or corporation in which his body was found shall cause it to be buried at the expense of the township or corporation in which he had a legal residence at the time of his death; if he had a legal residence in any other county of the state at the time of his death, the infirmary superintendent of the county in which his dead body was 

9. Sec. 3494, G. C., 108 O. L., Pt. 1, 274. 250

found shall cause it to be buried at the expense of the township or corporation in which he had a legal residence at the time of his death, but if he had no legal residence in the state, or his legal residence is unknown, such infirmary superintendent shall cause him to be buried at the expense of the county. 

MARKER AT GRAVE. It shall be the duty of such offi cials to provide at the grave of such person, a stone or concrete marker on which shall be inscribed the name and age of such person, if known, and the date of death.1

DISCUSSION: 

Where an indigent person is a legal resident of the county in this state, the expense of the burial of such person should be paid by the township or municipal corporation in which such person had a legal residence at the time of his death,2 if such person was not an inmate of a penal, reformatory, or benevolent or charitable institution of this state, and if the body is not claimed for the purpose of medical or surgical study or dissection as provided by Sec. 9984 G. C.3 If the legal residence is in a village, the township in which the village is located should pay the expense thereof.4 In the case of a city or township, the expense should be paid out of the poor fund if a levy has been made therefor. Tuberculosis patients dying in county sanitoriums should be paid by the county.5

Tuberculosis patients receiving home treatment should be paid by the township or municipality in which they are residents.6

Where an indigent person dies in a township or municipality in which he does not have a legal residence, and if a non-resident of the county, the infirmary superintendent of the county in which death occurred should cause such person to be buried? at the expense of the township or municipality in which such person had a legal residence at the time of his death.8 It is the duty of township or municipal officers to cause an indigent who dies. within their township or municipality to be buried and charge the expense to the township or municipality in which such person had a legal settlement if the legal settlement is within the same county.9

POOR RELIEF DISCUSSION: 

All persons who die in state institutions are now buried at the expense of the state.1

County charges2 including patients in a tuberculosis sanitorium³ and inmates of soldiers' home,4 should be buried at the expense of the county if indigent. Burial lots may be provided in either public or private cemeteries for such purposes.

SEC. 4436 G. C. MAINTENANCE OF PERSON CONFINED IN QUARANTINED HOUSE. 

When a house or other place is quarantined on account of contagious diseases, the board of health having jurisdiction shall provide for all persons confined in such house or place, food, fuel, and all other necessities of life, including medical attendance, medicine and nurses when necessary. The expenses so incurred, except those for disinfection, quarantine or other measures strictly for the protection of the public health, when properly certified by the president and clerk of the board of health, or health officer where there is no board of health, shall be paid by the person or persons quarantined, when able to make such payment, and when not, by the municipality or township in which quarantined.5

DISCUSSION: 

It is the duty of the board of health to determine what fuel, food, medical attendance, medicine and nursing are needed by persons quarantined and furnish the same to such persons, if it is necessary to do so.6 Such expense should be paid by the persons quarantined if able to do so, and if not by the township or municipality (including villages) in which quarantined.7 It is the duty of the officers of the township or municipality to determine whether such persons are able to pay or not, and such determination should be made when bills are presented for payment.8 

A physician who attends quarantined persons unable to pay therefor can recover from the municipality or township if the board of health fails to provide necessary medical service when notified.9

When a board of health furnished services for nonresidents of the county, the county commissioners of the 1.

Expenses incurred by a board of health for non-residents of the state for quarantined persons are properly chargeable to the township or municipality in which quarantined.

SEC. 4849 G. C. COMPULSORY SCHOOL AGE. 

A child between six and eighteen years of age is "of compulsory school age" for the purpose of this chapter; but the board of education of any district may by resolution raise the minimum compulsory school age of all children residing in the district to seven, subject to subsequent modification to six; and the compulsory school age of a child shall not commence until the beginning of the term of such schools, or other time in the school year fixed by the rules of the board of education of the district in which he resides. 

The parents, guardians or other persons who have the care of a child of compulsory school age shall instruct him, or cause him to be instructed as herein provided, unless he is employed on age and schooling certificate, or shall have been determined, as provided by law, to be mentally incapable of profiting substantially by further instruction.1

SEC. 4849-1 G. C. WHO AMENABLE TO LAWS RELAT- ING TO COMPULSORY EDUCATION. 

Every child actually resident in the state shall be amenable to the laws relating to compulsory education, and neither he nor the person in charge of him shall be excused from the operation of said laws or the penalties under them on the 

1. Sec. 4849, G. C., 120 O. L., 475, effective Sept. 16, 1943. 255

COMPULSORY EDUCATION 

ground that the child's residence is seasonal or that the parent of the child is a resident of another state or that the child has attended school for the legal period in another state.2 

SEC. 4851-7 G. C. APPEAL WHEN EXCUSE OR CERTIFI- CATE IS REFUSED. 

In case a superintendent of schools refuses to excuse a child from attendance at school for one of the reasons stated in section 4849-3 of the General Code, or refuses upon request to grant an age and schooling certificate as provided in section 4851 of the General Code, an appeal may be taken from such decision to the judge of the juvenile court of the county, upon the giving of bond, within ten days thereafter, to the approval of such judge, to pay the costs of appeal. His decision in the matter shall be final.3

DISCUSSION: 

This statute is silent as to who shall file the appeal. It is doubtful whether a child could do so on its own motion. 

The proper procedure would be for a parent, guardian or custodian of the child to file the same in the name of the child, as its best friend.

(b) When An Age and Schooling Certificate May Be 

Issued. (1) To a child over sixteen years of age who has satisfactorily passed a test for the completion of the work of the seventh grade. No such certificate can be issued unless the child has employment not prohibited by law.1 (2) To a child over fourteen years of age who has been declared incapable of profiting substantially by further instruction.2 (3) To a child over sixteen years of age who is unable to pass a test for completion of work in the seventh grade. No such certificate can issue unless such child has previously been a regular attendant at school; or is not sufficiently familiar with the English language; or that the child must provide for his own support or that the child is needed for the support of a parent or parents, or for the support or care of brothers or sisters. Written consent must be given by the juvenile judge and the state division of charities for release to provide support. In addition, such child must attend part time or evening classes as may be available to him.3 (4) To a child under sixteen years of age who has graduated from the twelfth grade.4 (5) To a child over fourteen years of age for part time or during vacation employment.5 (c) Employment of Minors. SEC. 4852-1 G. C. COUNTY ATTENDANCE OFFICER; 

COMPENSATION. 

Every county board of education shall employ a county attendance officer, and may employ or appoint such assistants as the board may deem advisable. The compensation and necessary traveling expenses of such attendance officer and assistants shall be paid out of the county board of education fund. With the consent and approval of the judge of the juvenile court, a probation officer of the court may be designated as the county attendance officer or as an assistant. The compensation of the probation officers of the juvenile court so designated shall be fixed and paid in the same manner as salaries of other probation officers of the juvenile court; their traveling expenses as attendance officers which would not be incurred as probation officers shall be paid out of the county board of education fund. In addition to the compensation here provided the county board of education may pay such additional compensation as it may deem advisable, to any probation officer designated as attendance officer and such additional amount shall be paid from the county board of education fund. The county attendance officer and assistants shall work under the direction of the county superintendent of schools. The authority of such attendance officer and assistants shall extend to all the local school districts which form the county school district. But this section shall not be interpreted to confine their authority to investigate employment to that within the county school district.10

DISCUSSION: 

The positions of deputy sheriff,11 court constable,12 6.

SEC. 4852-5 G. C. EXAMINATION OF CASES OF SUP- POSED TRUANCY; NOTICE TO PARENT. 

On the request of the superintendent of schools or the board of education or when it otherwise comes to his notice, the attendance officer shall examine into any case of supposed truancy within his district, and warn the child, if found truant, and his parent, guardian or other person in charge of him, in writing, of the legal consequences of truancy if persisted in. When any child of compulsory school age in violation of the provisions of law, is not attending school, the attendance officer shall notify the parent, guardian or other person in charge of such child of the fact, and require such parent, guardian or other person to cause the child to attend school forthwith; and it shall be the duty of the parent, guardian or other person in charge of the child so to cause its attendance at school. Upon the failure of the parent, guardian or other person in charge of the child to do so, the attendance officer shall make complaint against the parent, guardian or other person in charge of the child in any court of competent jurisdiction.5

DISCUSSION: 

The penalty for violating this section is found in Section 12974 G. C.

A conscientious objector who keeps his child out of school because it is required to partake in patriotic exercises including a salute to the flag is subject to the penalty of this section.6

SEC. 4852-6 G. C. WARNING TO CHILD, PARENT OR GUARDIAN; COMPLAINT. 

When any child, in violation of the provisions of section 4849-7 or 4849-8 of the General Code, is not attending a parttime school or class the attendance officer shall warn the child and his parent, guardian or other person in charge of him in writing of the legal consequences of his failure to attend such school or class. SEC. 4852-9 G. C. WHO DEEMED IN CHARGE OF CHILD. 

If a child is residing apart from its parents, and the parents are not residents of the given school district, the person in whose residence the child resides shall be deemed the person in charge of the child for the purpose of sections 4852-5, 4852-6 or 4852-8 of the General Code, and if several families are tenants in common of the residence in which the child resides and no one claims to be the person in charge of the child the court shall be judge of who is de facto in charge of the child.11

SEC. 4853-9 G. C. AUDITOR SHALL TRANSMIT AB- STRACT TO SUPERINTENDENT AND JUVENILE JUDGE. 

11. Sec. 4852-9, G. C., 120 O. L., 475, effective Sept. 16, 1943. 

1. Sec. 4853-9, G. C., 120 O. L., 475, effective Sept. 16, 1943. 

On or before the third Saturday of August the auditor of each county shall transmit to the superintendent of public instruction on blanks furnished by him a duly certified abstract of the enumeration returns made to him; and at the same time such auditor shall transmit to the juvenile judge of the county, duplicate transcripts of the list of crippled children residing in each school district of the county, as shown by said school enumeration reports, and, if upon examination and investigation of such reports, the juvenile judge has reason to believe the report is incomplete or inaccurate, he shall transmit a written order to the county auditor, directing him to procure an enumeration of all the crippled children, as provided in section 4853-12 of the General Code.1 

DISCUSSION: 

The purpose of this section is to discover crippled 262

children that may be aided under the provisions of 1639 et seq., G. C., and Sec. 1352-8 G. C.

SEC. 4853-10 G. C. JUVENILE JUDGE SHALL TRANSMIT COPY TO HEALTH COMMISSIONER; HEALTH COM- MISSIONER SHALL PROVIDE EXAMINATION. 

Within thirty days after receiving such duplicate transcripts of the list of crippled children from the auditor, each juvenile judge shall transmit a copy thereof to the health commissioner of each health district in his county, so far as the same contains the names of crippled children residing in the district of the respective health commissioners; and each health commissioner shall, within sixty days thereafter, provide examination of each crippled child on such list, and transmit to the juvenile judge and the Ohio Department of Health a report, on a form prescribed by the director of health, which shall be complete in describing such disability. 

Whenever such health commissioner is so directed by the state director of health, he shall make application in the juvenile court for the proper care, treatment, and education for such crippled child or children, as provided in section 1352-8 of the General Code, for the purpose of obtaining treatment and care for all crippled children in his health district.2

SEC. 7995 G. C. 

MUTUAL OBLIGATIONS. 

Husband and wife contract towards each other obligations of mutual respect, fidelity, and support.1

DISCUSSION: 

(a) Definition of Marriage. “Marriage, as distinguished from the agreement to marry and from the act of becoming married, is the civil status of one man and one woman united in law for life, for the discharge to each other and the community of the duties legally encumbent on those whose association is founded on the distinction of sex.2 

Such relationship is created by mutual agreement.3 

At common law, males of the age of fourteen and females twelve years of age could contract marriage,4 but under section 11181 G. C., the age has been fixed at eighteen and sixteen respectively. There are several exceptions. (b) History. This section and the sections following up to and including section 8004 G. C., constituted the first part of the Ohio "Married Women's Act" enacted in 1887.

Under this section a husband and wife contract toward each other obligations of mutual respect, fidelity and support. The husband is the head of the family and may choose any reasonable place or mode of living and the wife must conform thereto.5 

Sec. 7995, G. C., R. S. 3108.

Black's Law Dictionary. 

Waymire vs. Jetmore, 22 O. S. 274. 1. 2. 3. 

4. Shafer vs. State, 20 Ohio 1. 

5. Mark vs. Mark, 145 O. S. 301, 61 N. E. (2d) 595. 265

(c) Actions Generally. A married woman may bring an action in her own name, such as a claim for loss against a carrier,6 for services in giving special care to an invalid in the name of her husband,7 or for services rendered outside the home.8 (d) Personal Injuries to Wife. A husband had no individual right of action for an injury sustained by his wife,9 as she may bring an action in her own behalf, but he may recover for loss of her services, and if death occurs, only between the time of injury and death.10 A husband is bound to pay for medical services rendered his wife, and she cannot recover the expense thereof in an action for her personal injuries.11 He can recover for necessary medical, surgical and other expenses incurred by him12 and such action may be brought within a period of four years under the provisions of Sec. 11224 G. C.13 (e) Personal Injuries to Husband. A wife has no right of action against a person for the loss of the consortium of her husband caused by personal injuries sustained by him through the negligence of such person¹ but she may maintain an action against one who sells her husband morphine against her protests,2 or for unlawfully supplying her husband with intoxicating liquors.3 

(f) Enticing, Alienating and Criminal Conversation. 

Husband and wife are entitled to the affection, society, cooperation and aid of each other in every conjugal relation, and either may maintain an action for damages against any one who wrongfully and maliciously interferes with the marital relationship and thereby deprives one of the society, affection and consortium of the other.4 

The right of consortium means the right to enjoy the society, companionship, conjugal affection, fellowship and assistance of the spouse, as distinguished from services of a practical nature. The common-law rule does not contemplate a right of action based solely and exclusively upon loss of consortium.5 

Consortium Involved

The marital relation is invaded, and a cause of action for alienation of affection accrues whenever a third person by enticement, seduction or other wrongful and in- 

6. Pullman Co. vs. Laws, 10 Ohio App., 344, 31 O. C. A., 385.

Flandermeyer vs. Cooper, 85 O. S., 327, 98 N. E., 102. 

5. Kelley vs. Bouche, 6 Ohio Sup. 327, 21 O. O. 244. 266

tentional interference with the marriage relation, deprives either husband or wife of the consortium of the other.6

In order to recover for alienation of affections, some act of restraint? or malice8 must be shown. If one either actively or passively connived at defendant's improper relations with a spouse, or if the separation was due to the neglect or want of support9 or other conduct of the plaintiff,10 no recovery can be had. If a husband voluntarily bestows his affections on another without any act on the part of the defendant, which results in alienation, no action lies.11 

Where a wife's parents have an honest and reasonable belief that it was necessary to house and protect their daughter while in fact there was no such necessity, they are not liable.1

An action may be maintained without proof of actual separation if there has been loss of consortium2 and although a divorce has been obtained by the person whose affections are alleged to be alienated.3 7. 8. 9. 10. 11. 1. 2. 3. 4. 5.

Both husband and wife may maintain an action for criminal conversation4 without proof of seduction or alienation of affections.5

Under Section 11224 G. C., a suit for alienation of affections may be brought within four years from the time the action accrued.6

A petition for alienating affections should allege the acts complained of as mere averments of conspiracy, ill will, and malice are conclusions of law.7

The mere fact a father acts as next friend for his minor son in instituting a divorce action does not prompt the inference that he had prevailed upon the son to begin the suit.8

SEC. 7996 G. C. HUSBAND IS THE HEAD OF THE FAMILY. 

The husband is the head of the family. He may choose any reasonable place or mode of living, and the wife must conform thereto.9 9. 10. 

DISCUSSION: 

A husband has the right to select the place of abode, and it is the duty of the wife to submit thereto, provided he acts in good faith, with due regard to the wife's comfort, health and welfare.10 It is not reasonable, however, to go from a place where opportunities for schooling and employment exist to one where these advantages are not afforded.11 11. 12.

A wife who leaves the home of her husband without just cause, without his consent and against his wish, and takes a minor child with her, cannot compel the husband to support her or such child if he offers to maintain a home for them.12 

Sec. 7996, G. C., R. S. 3109. 

Burke vs. Burke, 36 Ohio App., 551, 173 N. E., 637; Mark vs. Mark, 145 O. S., 301, 61 N. E. (2d) 595. 

Imler vs Imler, 8 O. N. P. (N. S.), 409. 

In re Poage, 87 O. S., 72. 269

SEC. 7997 G. C. DUTY OF HUSBAND TO SUPPORT FAMILY. 

2. 3. 

The husband must support himself, his wife, and his minor children out of his property or by his labor. If he is unable to do so, the wife must assist him so far as she is able.5 4. 

Upon the death or legal disability of the father, the mother becomes the head of the family.1 Upon desertion, the wife may occupy homestead and claim homestead rights.2 5.

In case of a disagreement between husband and wife over the religious training of a child, the wishes of the wife should prevail.3

A minor female upon her marriage loses her legal settlement acquired through her parents and obtains a new settlement derivatively which is that of her husband; and she may not acquire a new legal setlement even if her husband is in the armed forces until termination of the marital relationship.4

DISCUSSION: 

(a) Husband. Under this section it is the husband's duty to support himself and his wife so long as he is able so to do. The statutes do not require a husband to provide other than present support for his wife and he may dispose of his personal property during his life-time without the consent of his wife.6

When a husband is unable to support himself, he can compel his wife to support him.7 This rule applies to the husband's funeral expenses.8 The legal duty of a surviving spouse to take charge of the decedent's body and give it a decent burial if the decedent dies without leaving an estate available for the payment of burial expenses, applies to a minor widow equally with adult spouses, and a contract by a minor widow with an undertaker for burial expenses is not voidable.9 A stepfather is not liable for the support of his step children unless he assumes such responsibility.10

A wife cannot recover from her husband's estate for nursing him while he was a chronic invalid.11 (b) Wife. A husband is liable for all necessaries furnished a wife, and such necessaries are not limited to the bare necessities of life.12 Thus, a rug, purchased at a price of $120 to replace one that had been in use for eleven years and was rather badly worn and faded, for a home valued at $8500 was held to be a necessary, for which the wife could bind the husband if the husband was earning $70 a week.13 Necessaries include medical services14 and funeral expenses.15 The obligation to support a wife is a debt which a wife can enforce against a husband's property.1

The wife cannot be held for such necessaries except by her expressed agreement2 although her silence will amount to an acceptance where she is informed that she will be held liable in the future.3

The obligation of a husband to support his wife and family is not discharged by his continuing physical disability, if he has financial means to support them.5 

He cannot be held for his wife's attorney fees in a divorce case which was dismissed by agreement.6 

A married woman's funeral and last sickness expenses are debts of her estate and her executor may properly allow and pay them.7

ricisti

A husband is not his wife's agent as a matter of law,I but where the home belongs to the wife, the husband in purchasing an article therefor may be held to have acted as his wife's agent.2 A husband who buys food or medicine is presumed to be acting as husband and father and not as agent for his wife.3 

Where a husband furnishes his wife with necessaries and plenty of cash, he cannot be held for goods purchased on his credit by his wife on an implied agency4 but where a wife is injured in an accident and sends for a physician, she does so as the agent for the husband.5 In the absence of specific authority, a wife cannot institute a criminal rroceeding in the name of the husband so as to make him liable for damages.6 A wife acts as the agent for the husband in engaging medical services for him while under legal disability.7 But a wife who drives her husband's automobile with his consent is not his agent as a matter of law and to hold a husband as a principal other facts showing agency or joint enterprise must be established.8

Abandonment During Pregnancy 

It is no defense to a prosecution for abandoning a pregnant wife if the husband did not know she was pregnant, if it was his intent to abandon her.9 (c) Children. The father's liability to support his children is primary and that of the mother is secondary. A wife is not required to support the children unless the husband is unable to do so.10

The obligation to support children includes adopted children11 and illegitimate children under eighteen12 but not step-children.13 It ceases at his death and he may entirely disinherit his children.14 1.

SEC. G. C. INTEREST IN PROPERTY OF THE OTHER. 

Neither husband nor wife has any interest in the property of the other, except as mentioned in the next preceding section, the right to dower and to remain in the mansion house after the death of either, as provided by law; and neither can be excluded from the other's dwelling, except upon a decree or order of injunction made by a court of competent jurisdiction.11

DISCUSSION: 

(a) Interests Generally. One who conveys real estate to his children of a first marriage without consideration other than love or affection, upon becoming engaged to marry, does not act in good faith, and his second wife is entitled to dower in such real estate. 12 A conveyance of personal property to alienate a spouse's interest will be set aside.13

Under this section neither the husband nor wife has any interest in the property of the other except the present right of support, and the rights given by statute to a surviving spouse in the remaining property of the deceased spouse and gifts to children of a prior marriage will not be invalidated.14

A wife may permit her husband to sue and recover for services performed by her,15 but a husband cannot compromise a settlement of his wife's claim for personal injuries.16 

Neither husband nor wife can be excluded from the other's dwelling except upon a decree or order of Court.17 (b) Resulting Trusts. A resulting trust exists in favor of a person whose earnings have been invested in realty in the spouse's name under the understanding that the property is to belong to the person furnishing the money.1 There must be a contract, oral or written to that effect.2 If the spouse dies willing such person a life estate only, such person is entitled to a reconveyance.3 To establish a trust, evidence beyond a reasonable doubt is required and it must clearly establish its terms and conditions.4

A spouse has no vested interest in a certificate of insurance, and another beneficiary may be substituted

Where property has been accumulated by joint enterprise under a void marriage, the alleged husband cannot recover all of the property which he placed in his alleged wife's name.6 

Tenants in Common (c) Property Acquired in Joint Names. Estates in joint tenancy do not exist in Ohio, and where property stands in the name of two or more persons, they are tenants in common.7 Where title is taken in the joint names of husband and wife, the presumption that they are tenants in common, may be overcome by evidence that one party furnished the entire purchase price, and that the other exercises no control over the property and committed acts in the nature of a disclaimer.8

Joint accounts may be legally established, payable to either or the survivor.9

Where property is held by husband and wife which is mortgaged and one party pays off the mortgage, such party is thereby subrogated to the rights of the mortgagee.10

The acquiescence of both parties is necessary to pass title where property is held by husband and wife as tenants in common.1 

For a history of wife's property rights, see Dillingham vs. Dillingham, 28 O. C. A., 49, 30 O. C. D. 6.

Each Spouse Bound 

Where a person makes a sale of his spouse's property, and if such spouse accepts the benefits of such contract, such spouse is bound by such person's representation.6 

A person need not specifically authorize a spouse to act for such person in employing a real estate agent to sell real estate belonging to such person, as it may be conferred by implication.7 (g) Ante-nuptial Settlements. Persons who are engaged to be married occupy relations of trust and confidence to each other.1 Consequently, where an ante-nuptial contract is in contemplation, it is the duty of the dominant party to make a full and clear statement of all facts which relate to the subject matter of the contract.2 

Where an ante-nuptial contract is entered into, it will be enforced where it is fair, reasonable and just, as between the parties under the circumstances of the case.3 

But if no disclosure is made, and the provision is much less than that which a party would have by law received, in the absence of a contract, at death, such contract may be avoided.4 

Where a man agreed to pay $2500.00 while he was worth $55,000.00, it will be set aside if he did not act in good faith with a full disclosure of his property, and in such case the burden is on his heirs to prove his good faith and that a full disclosure was made.5 

And, likewise, where a man worth $131,000.00 executed an ante-nuptial contract with a girl without means of business experience, or knowledge of the value of property, it will be set aside as a fraud if it only provided for a yearly annuity of $500.00 and a lump settlement of $10,000.00, at death.6

Ante-Nuptial Agreement Enforced 

An unperformed ante-nuptial agreement may be enforced against the legal representative of a decedent,7 and if made a lien in the contract it has a preference over general creditors. An unperformed contract may be avoided, and a spouse may then claim her allowances and statutory rights,8 but a wife cannot maintain an action against her husband for personal injuries even though

they were received and action begun before the marriage and an ante-nuptial contract can not save the action.9 

An ante-nuptial contract cannot be avoided on the ground of concealment of facts where the concealed facts did not affect the interest of the survivor.10

A court of equity will not cancel for unfairness an antenuptial contract, made during engagement and ratified six years after marriage, whereby the husband and wife each agreed to take nothing from the other's estate, where no evidence is presented as to the respective values of the estate so as to show disparity in the rights surrendered, and the major portion, if not all, of the deceased spouse's estate consisted of real property situated in the county where they resided.11

When a woman in an ante-nuptial contract, reserves the unlimited right to dispose of her property by will, that right applies to a will whether it ante-dates or postdates the contract; the date of the will's execution being immaterial, as, in either instance, the effective date is the same, i. e. her death. When a man in an ante-nuptial contract releases all rights, interests, etc., in his intended wife's estate that he may have at her death, which "accrues or arises by reason of said (his) marriage," the legacy given him by her in a will ante-dating the contract "accrues and arises" not from the marriage but from the will itself,12 

A year's allowance may be barred by ante-nuptial agreement13 and so may the set-off, provided for under 

Sec. 10509-54 G. C.

An ante-nuptial agreement should be construed most favorably to the woman, where ambiguous.1 

An ante-nuptial contract is no bar to being awarded alimony in a divorce case, if a divorce is awarded for the other's aggression even though the contract provided that if the parties ceased to live together they should have no interest in each other's property.2 

Where a will has a provision in lieu of the provision in an ante-nuptial contract, electing to take under the will invalidates the contract.3 

Under an ante-nuptial contract, a note payable at death of the maker cannot be enforced by the personal representative of the spouse's estate, if the spouse died before the maker.4 The declaratory judgment act can be used to determine the validity of an ante-nuptial contract.5 9.

SEC. G. C. 

HUSBAND AND WIFE CAN CONTRACT WITH ONE ANOTHER. 

A husband or wife may enter into any engagement or transaction with the other, or with any other person, which either might if unmarried; subject, in transaction between themselves, to the general rules which control the actions of persons occupying confidential relations with each other.6 6. 7.

DISCUSSION: 

This section authorizes a husband and wife to enter into any agreement or transaction subject to the next succeeding section.7 They may form a partnerships and the husband may make a binding contract with his wife to pay her for nursing him while recovering from injuries inflicted by third persons.9 10. 11. 1. 2. 3. 4. 5. 1. 2. 

A husband and wife have no greater power to contract with each other than unmarried persons possess. They cannot contract concerning an expectancy which is within a vested or a conjugal interest.10 But release of dower and distributive share in the property of the other is not considered release of a mere expectancy.11 

A note given by a husband to a wife for money borrowed is collectible.2 A mortgage likewise is valid.3 But where a wife draws her money out of a bank to improve her husband's lands4 and pay his debts, there is no implied promise to repay.5

A conveyance by a husband to the wife to secure a debt does not extinguish her dower.1

Where a wife furnishes money to her husband to pay indebtedness to a creditor to whom he owes two overdue bills, on one of which his wife was surety, the creditor will be required to apply the payment on the debts on which the wife is surety if he knew the payment was made with the wife's money.2

There can be no recovery upon notes given by the hus- 

Owes Before and After

Debts due to a woman are not extinguished by her marriage with the debtor.1

Husband and wife may make gifts to one another, but such gifts are carefully scrutinized if they affect their persons.4 33457 A wife's assent to her husband's use and re-investment of her property for over thirty years will be construed that a gift was intended.5

Where notes are assigned by a husband to a wife without consideration, she can recover thereon.6 It requires a high degree of proof to set aside a gift from husband to wife if third persons are not affected.7 

They may assign rights due one to the other.8

Persons occupying confidential relations include attorney and client, physician and patient, priest and confessor, trustee and cestui, guardian and ward, executor and estate, etc. 

Consequently all transactions between husband and wife will be closely scrutinized. If made with fraudulent intent, they may be set aside.9

Gifts made by one to the other are valid if not made with fraudulent intent.10 At common law a husband could not make a gift of real estate to his wife during coverture without the intervention of a trustee.11 

SEC. 8000 G. C. CANNOT ALTER THEIR LEGAL RELA- TIONS. 

A husband and wife cannot by any contract with each other alter their legal relations, except that they may agree to an immediate separation, and make provisions for the support of either of them and their children during the separation.12 

DISCUSSION: 

The term "legal relations" comprises not only the purely marital relations existing between husband and wife, but also the property provisions which the law gives to each upon the death of one.13 

(a) Separation Agreements. A separation agreement followed by separation, in which the wife releases her right to a year's support and other allowances, out of the

A mere resumption of marital relations is not sufficient.4 

A separation agreement is construed most favorable to the woman if ambiguous.5 An agreement carried into a divorce decree can be construed under the declaratory judgment act.6 (b) Effect of Divorce. This section providing that husband and wife cannot contract to alter their legal relations except that they may agree to an immediate separation and make provision for the support of either of them and their children during separation has no reference to any rights that either may relinquish upon the bringing of suit for divorce against the other, but contemplates only the relation that exists when they are living separate and apart.7

A separation agreement providing for the future support of a minor child of the parties and approved by the court in a divorce decree, may not in the absence of fraud or mistake be subsequently modified by the court so as to lessen the amount of support for such minor child.8 

A separation agreement entered into after a divorce suit has been instituted by the wife providing for temporary and permanent alimony until contract should be abrogated by the parties or by order of the court is valid when a divorce is secured by the wife even though the agreement is not incorporated in the decree and was not submitted to the court by the wife as she agreed to do.9 (c) Children. The parents of a minor child, may by contract with a third person, or by their conduct, deprive themselves or forfeit their right to the care and custody of the child.10 (d) Joint Wills. Under the provisions of this section, a surviving spouse who executes a joint will with her consort during coverture can elect not to take under such will, even if such will is made in pursuance of contract.11

SEC. 8001 G. C. MAY TAKE AND HOLD PROPERTY. 

A married person may take, hold and dispose of property, real or personal, the same as if unmarried.12

DISCUSSION: 

A married woman's property, including proceeds from life insurance, is liable for her debts.13

The right to hold property includes the right to bring an action against a carrier for the loss of her separate property.14

SEC. 8002 G. C. NOT ANSWERABLE FOR ACTS OF THE 

OTHER.

Neither husband nor wife, as such, is answerable for the acts of the other.15

DISCUSSION: 

This section is subject to the relation of principal and agent, or master and servant, or joint enterprise. A husband or wife may be bound for the acts of the other if such relationship exists but the fact that a husband gave his wife permission to use his automobile to go to church does not make the husband liable for damages on the ground of master and servant for negligent injuries 12. 13. 14. 15.

Pullman Co. vs. Laws, 10 Ohio App., 344.

SEC. G. C. SUPPORT OF WIFE WHEN HUSBAND NEGLECTS TO DO SO. 

1. inflicted by the wife while so driving the automobile even though she took her child with her.1

If the husband neglects to make adequate provision for the support of his wife, any other person, in good faith, may supply her with necessaries for her support, and recover the reasonable value thereof from the husband.6

DISCUSSION: 

A husband is primarily responsible for necessaries purchased by a wife for the family.7 Necessaries include medical services,8 and funeral expenses even though parties are separated.9

Neither husband nor wife can be held liable for the crime of the other unless a party to it.2 4+23+ The presumption that a married woman who commits a criminal act in the presence of her husband acts under his coercion is only prima facie, and she may be prosecuted if it is shown she acted voluntarily.3 A husband or wife cannot be prosecuted for larceny from the other,4 or for malicious destruction of each other's property during coveture if not separated.5

SEC. 8004 G. C. WHEN WIFE ABANDONS HUSBAND. 

If the wife abandons the husband, he is not liable for her support until she offers to return, unless she was justified, by his misconduct, in abandoning him.1

DISCUSSION: 

One who furnishes necessaries to a wife who has left her husband has the burden of proving that she did so under justifiable circumstances.2

SEC. 8023 G. C. AGE OF MAJORITY. 

All persons of the age of twenty-one years and upward, who are under no legal disability, shall be capable of contracting respecting goods, chattels, lands, tenements, and any other matter or thing which may be the legitimate subject of a contract, and, to all intents and purposes be of full age.1 2. 3.

Rights and Duties of Parents Separated or Divorced. 

Court to Determine Custody of Children. 

Order of Court. 

DISCUSSION: 

(a) Age of Majority. Due to the fact that the law does not recognize fractional parts of a day,2 a minor becomes of age the day before the twenty-first birthday.3

A statute changing the age of majority is not retroactive, although it has been held that a female who became eighteen before the age of majority was raised to twenty-one, and thereafter and before she became twenty-one conveyed land by deed could repudiate her conveyance.4 (b) Disabilities in General. A minor cannot change his legal settlement from the place where his father is legally settled, unless such minor has been abandoned.1 4. 1. 

An infant cannot give a proxy for voting stock,2 become a member of a mutual burial association,3 appoint an agent or attorney to make contracts,4 or consent to the building of a street railway abutting on property in which he has an interest,5 but he may rescind a partner-

When Minor Becomes Soldier 

Where a minor enlists in the Ohio National Guard or in the Federal Army by misrepresenting his age, and receives pay under such enlistment, he becomes a soldier subject to all the rules and regulations, the same as an adult; and habeas corpus will not release him for violating military law.8 (c) Contracts. All contracts of an infant, except those acts which the law permits or requires, are voidable at the infant's option,9 even though the adversary party believes that such minor is of full age.10 Minors have only such right to contract as the State awards them.11 An infant who avoids a contract can recover for services rendered under the contract on a quantuum meruit basis, after part performance.12

A voidable contract even if prejudicial may be ratified by an infant after he reaches the age of majority.13

It has been held that an infant may avoid a marriage contract, the same as any other executory contract.14 (d) Funeral Expenses of Spouse. A minor may avoid a promise to pay for funeral expenses out of the proceeds of life insurance, 15 but a minor widow may not avoid her contract for the burial of her husband when he dies without leaving an estate.16 (e) Children. A minor cannot relinquish her right to custody of her minor child by contract, except in cases of adoption.17 (f) Necessaries. An infant is liable for necessaries furnished him, but only for the executed part of the contract.18 It does not include care and medical attention furnished on the credit of the parents of an infant.19

Endorser of Minor's Note 

(g) Mortgages and Notes. A mortgage or note executed by a minor to secure a debt, except for necessaries may be repudiated.4 

An infant who has for ten years after reaching his majority remained mute as to any disaffirmance of a note given while an infant thereby deprives himself of any right to now assert that the note was void because of his infancy.5 

An endorsee on an infant's note can be held without notice as upon an original undertaking.6 (h) Deeds. The deed of a minor transmits title until disaffirmed,7 but it must be legally executed.8 A minor spouse who executes a deed in conjunction with the owner, cannot be deprived of the right of dower.9 (i) Estoppel. Generally the doctrine of estoppel is not applicable to infants.10 Consequently a minor who fraudulently represents his age, and sells land,11 or purchases an automobile12 is not estopped from avoiding his contract; but in making a purchase of personalty in such a case, and the contract is fair, and the infant sues to recover without restoring the property, a vendor may counter-claim for his damages.13 The other party may also avoid a contract on the ground of misrepresentation.14

But where there has been no misrepresentation as to age, no suit for counter-claim can be maintained.1 

Where an infant executes a mortgage against his real estate, and after becoming of age, disaffirms such mortgage by selling the same real estate to a third person, the mortgage cannot thereafter be ratified.2

INFANTS (j) Time for Ratification or Avoidance. Contracts of an infant, except those authorized by law, are voidable at the election of the infant at any time before or within a reasonable time after reaching his majority.3 

A minor who purchases an automobile and makes payments thereon for six months after he becomes of age is bound by his contract.4

Silence Not Ratification 

Silence or acquiescence does not amount to ratification5 but a promise to perform made after arriving at full age is a ratification.6 

Ratification after arriving at the age of majority is binding and a minor cannot thereafter disaffirm.7 

A conveyance of realty by an infant may be disallowed at any time within twenty-one years from the time he reaches his majority8 as provided by Sec. 11219 G. C. (k) Avoidance. The right to disaffirm is a personal right of the infant, and is not available to others.9

An infant may avoid a conveyance of realty by resorting to a court of equity for cancellation or by an action in ejectment10 within twenty-one years after he reaches his majority.11

A conveyance to a third party after reaching majority12 or of a mortgage made during minority13 is a disaffirmance of a sale. Likewise, a partition action is a disaffirmance.14

Where property is purchased by a minor for another1 or where his title amounts to a trust2 he cannot disaffirm a conveyance thereof after he attains his majority. See also "Estoppel' and "Time For Ratification and 

Avoidance" supra.

Thus an infant is liable for selling property held by him as bailee,3 and for negligence in handling a gun which resulted in discharging it and causing injury.4

9. 10. 1. an infant sues to recover money paid upon the purchase of personalty such as an automobile, without restoring the property, a vendor may counter-claim for his damages if the contract is fair and the infant misrepresents his age when the purchase was made.7 10. 11. 12.

The liability of an infant for his torts is not affected by the fact that they were committed under the express order of or by the authority of his parent or guardian.8 

An infant cannot be held for the torts of one impliedly acting for him, because of the inability of a minor to contract,9 but he may be liable for torts which he procures another to commit.10

An infant is generally liable for malicious or intentional torts such as libel or slander if he has arrived at such age that malice and intent may be fairly imputed to him. But the measure of his capacity in that regard or in the case of negligence is a question for the jury.2 

Generally it may be stated that an infant is not liable in tort for breach of warranty,3 for fraud connected with a contract,4 for negligence connected with a contract,5 or for failure to perform a duty imposed by his contract of bailment.6 But he is liable in cases of bailment if the tort is independent of the breach of his contract of bailment,7 for fraud not connected with his contract,8 for negligence for failure to exercise such care as his age, intelligence, capacity, discretion and experience requires,9 and for malicious and intentional torts such as libel and slander if he has arrived at such age that such malice and intent may be fairly imputed to him.10 

The recovery against an infant for tort is limited to compensatory damages.11 8.

SEC. 8023-1 G. C. AGE OF MAJORITY FOR SERVICE MEN. 

Notwithstanding any provision of this chapter or any other provision of the law to the contrary, any person who is eligible for a loan under the servicemen's readjustment act of 1944 or any amendments thereto, whether or not said person or his or her spouse is a minor, is authorized and empowered, in his or her own name and without any order of court or the intervention of a guardian or trustee, to execute any and all contracts, notes, mortgages, conveyances, and instruments, to take title to real property, or to borrow money thereon and to do all other acts necessary to secure to such person, all rights and benefits under the servicemen's readjustment act of 1944 or any amendments and regulations thereto, in as full and ample manner as if such minor or his or her spouse had attained the age of twenty-one years. Any person so eligible for such loan or his or her spouse, shall not, by reason only of such minority, be incompetent to acquire title to property by contract or to borrow thereon and any contract, note, mortgage, release of dower, or other instrument made in connection with acquiring title to real estate or making such loan shall not be voidable on the grounds of minority of said person or his or her spouse.

Any person who has qualified under the provisions of the servicemen's readjustment act of 1944 or any amendments and regulations thereto and has secured a loan thereunder and has taken title to real property, under the provisions of said act, shall be eligible to dispose of said property by deed or other conveyance, notwithstanding the fact that said person or his or her spouse may be a minor and any such deed or other conveyance shall not be voidable on the grounds of minority of said person or his or her spouse.13

SEC. 8031 G. C. PARENT MAY BE SUMMONED BEFORE PROBATE COURT. 

Repealed.14

SEC. 8032 G. C. RIGHTS AND DUTIES OF PARENTS SEPARATED OR DIVORCED. 

When husband and wife are living separate and apart from each other, or are divorced and the question as to the care, custody and control of the offspring of their marriage is brought before a court of competent jurisdiction in this state, they shall stand upon an equality as to the care, custody and control of such offspring, so far as it relates to their being either father or mother thereof.15

DISCUSSION: 

This section does not apply where one parent is de- 

13. Sec. 8023-1 G. C., 121 O. L., 414, effective July 10, 1945. 

121 O. L. H., 463, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 14.

INFANTS 

ceased,16 nor can it be construed to relieve the father of his primary obligation to support his minor children as provided by Sec. 7997, G. C.17

The husband and wife stand on an equality and the court determines custody from the evidence.18 

The trial court, although dismissing the wife's petition for alimony and the husband's petition for divorce, nevertheless has jurisdiction under this section upon expressly finding that the husband has deserted his family and is residing elsewhere, to award custody of the minor children to the wife and to make an order providing for their support, the journal entry not showing dismissal of the custody and support portion of plaintiff's petition.1 

A child has a right enforceable in a court of law against one who has invaded and taken from said child the support and maintenance of its father, as well as damages of other rights which arise out of the family relationship and which have been destroyed or defeated by a wrongdoing third party.2

For a discussion of the liability of a husband to support a child awarded to his divorced wife, see 7 O. S. L. 

J. 446. See discussion under Sec. 8033, G. C.

SEC. 8033 G. C. COURT TO DETERMINE CUSTODY OF CHILDREN. 

Upon hearing the testimony of either or both of such parents, corroborated by other proof, the court shall decide which one of them shall have the care, custody and control of such offspring, taking into account that which would be for their best interests, except that, if such children be fourteen years of age or more, they may be allowed to choose which parent they prefer to live with, unless the court shall find that the parent so selected is unfitted to take charge of such children, in which event the court shall determine which parent shall have custody of such children. The above provisions permitting children to choose the parent with whom they desire to live, also shall apply to proceedings for modification of the former orders of the court, fixing the custody thereof, as in original actions. Provided that if the court finds with respect to any child under eighteen years of age that neither parent is a suitable person to have the custody of such child, the court shall, with the consent of the juvenile court, certify a copy of its findings, together with so much of the record and such further information in narrative form or otherwise, as the court deems necessary or the juvenile court may request, to the juvenile court for further proceedings according to law, and thereupon the juvenile court shall have exclusive 

16. In re Ramsey, 9 O. L. R., 231. 

17. Kintner vs. Kintner, 78 Ohio App., 324, 65 N. E. (2d) 156. 18. 

Parker vs. Parker, 28 O. L. A., 49. 1. 

Matthews vs. Matthews, 37 O. L. A., 283. 

2. Daily vs. Parker, 32 O. O., 223 (U. S. C. C. of App., 7th Ct.) 294

jurisdiction respecting such child. This section shall apply to pending actions.3

DISCUSSION: 

The privilege of a child 14 years of age or more, who is the subject of a custody proceeding under this section, to choose the parent with which he wishes to live, exists only where the court finds that both parents have capacity to properly care for the child.

A finding that changing the custody of a child from the father to the mother would not be for the best interest of the child and that the child would be immediately removed from this state is equivalent to a finding that the mother lacked the capacity to care properly for the child within the territorial jurisdiction of the court.4 

This section is mandatory and an election when made by a child must be respected unless the parent chosen is unfit to have custody, in which case the court will select the proper custodian. The burden to show unfitness is upon the person seeking modification of a decree awarding custody at the child's election. The court can refuse to hear testimony regarding a parent's habits earlier than one year preceding a hearing on an application to modify an award of custody on a child's election.5

Both parents are on an equal footing with respect to the right of custody of their minor children, and within this limitation the court will take into account that which would be for the best interest of the children, but a court may not award the custody to any other person unless a finding is made of the unfitness of the parents to have the care, custody and control of their children.6 4. 5. 3. 

(b) Paramount Right of a Parent to Custody of a 

Child. As a general rule, parents are entitled to the custody of their minor children; and when the mother being dead it is found that the father is a suitable person and the fitness of the home is not in dispute, and such father is able and willing to support and care for his child, the father's right of custody is paramount to that of all other persons. A parent has a right to the custody of his child against all other persons unless and until it is shown that the parent has relinquished his right to custody by contract, forfeited it by abandonment, or has lost it because of his unfitness or inability to provide a suitable home for his child. When, upon the death of the mother, the father allows a maternal aunt to take custody of his
