SEC. 8034 G. C. ORDER OF COURT. 

The court may order either or both parents to support or help support such children, whoever be their custodian, and also may make any just and reasonable order or decree, permitting the parent who is deprived of such care, custody and control of children to visit them at such time and under such conditions as the court may direct. The juvenile court shall have exclusive jurisdiction to enter such orders in any case certified to such court from another court.8 

DISCUSSION: 

Such order continues until a child reaches the age of majority.9 

SEC. 8034-1 G. C. 

CERTIFICATION OF AWARD OF CUS- TODY OF MINOR CHILDREN TO JUVENILE COURT. 

Any court other than a juvenile court, having jurisdiction in any case respecting the care, custody or support of a child under eighteen years of age may, on its own motion or on motion of any interested party, with the consent of the juvenile court, certify the record in the case or so much thereof and such further information in narrative form or otherwise as the court deems necessary or the juvenile court may request, to the juvenile court for the further proceedings according to law; and thereupon the juvenile court shall have exclusive jurisdiction respecting such child. This section shall apply to pending actions.

In any case where a common pleas court, or other court having jurisdiction, has made an award of the custody or an order for support or both of a minor child or children, the jurisdiction of such court shall not abate upon the death of the person awarded custody of said child or children, but shall continue for all purposes during the minority of said child or children and such court, upon its own motion or the motion of either parent or upon the motion of an interested person, acting in behalf of the child or children, may proceed to make further disposition of the case in the best interests of the child or children, or if such child or children are under eighteen years of age, the court may certify the same to the juvenile court of any county in this state for further proceedings thereunder according to law provided the consent of said juvenile court is first obtained. After certification of such case to a juvenile court, the jurisdiction of the common pleas court, or other court having jurisdiction shall cease, except as to any and all payments of alimony due for the spouse and support payments due and unpaid for such child or children. at the time of the certification.10

C., as originally passed, and prior to 1937 amendments, the court of common pleas, upon certifying to the juvenile court the matter of the custody and support of minor children of a divorced couple and order of support for further proceedings, retained no jurisdiction to entertain a motion by the wife based on the order of the common pleas court for support payments alleged to be in arrears.11

SEC. 8035 G. C. 

APPEAL. 

An appeal to higher court may be had upon appellant giving bond with one good surety to the adverse party, approved by the court from whose decree appealed, in a sum to be determined by it, but in no case to be less than accrued costs and a fair estimate of accruing costs. The court before whom such case is heard may decree costs of such proceeding against either party, or divide them in any proportion.7

SEC. 10070 G. C. APPOINTMENT OF HUMANE AGENTS. 

Such societies may appoint agents who are residents of the county or municipality for which the appointment is made, for the purpose of prosecuting any person guilty of an act of cruelty to persons or animals, who may arrest any person found violating any provision of this chapter, or any other law for protecting persons or animals or preventing acts of cruelty thereto. Upon making such arrest, such agent shall convey the person so arrested before some court or magistrate having jurisdiction of the offense, and there forthwith make complaint on oath or affirmation of the offense.1

SEC. 10071 G. C. APPROVAL OF APPOINTMENTS. 

All appointments by such societies under the next preceding section shall have the approval of the mayor of the city or village for which they are made. If the society exists outside of a city or village, appointments shall be approved by the probate judge of the county for which they are made. The mayor or probate judge shall keep a record of such appointments.2

DISCUSSION: 

The probate judge has discretion to determine whether the person named is a proper person and also whether there is such necessity for his appointment as will justify the payment of the expense out of the public treasury.3 

Such power also includes the determination of the necessity for the continuance of the service of an agent.4

SEC. 10081 G. C. MAY REMOVE CHILD FROM PARENTS. 

When an officer or agent of a humane society organized under this chapter, deems it for the best interest of a child, because of cruelty inflicted upon it, or because of its surroundings, that it be removed from the possession and control of the parents, or persons having charge thereof, such officer or agent may take possession of the child summarily, and shall immediately file a complaint in the juvenile court concerning such child and such court shall have full jurisdiction

SEC. 10512-9 G. C. 

'ADOPTION CODE"; DEFINITIONS. 

This act shall be known and may be cited as the "adoption code." As used in this "adoption code," unless otherwise indicated: (a) The term "child" shall mean any person under twentyone years of age. (b) The term "organization" shall mean any institution, public or private, and any private association, society, agency, person or persons located or operating in the state of Ohio, incorporated or unincorporated (,) having among its functions the furnishing of protective services or care for, or the placement of children in foster homes or elsewhere; and the 301

term "certified organization" shall mean any such organization holding a certificate in full force and effect, issued pursuant to section 1352-1 of the General Code, authorizing such organization to place children for adoption. (c) The term "permanent custody" shall mean custody which by the terms of the commitment or agreement, shall not, unless otherwise provided by law, terminate on or before a definite date prior to the majority of the child, or which is not subject to termination at any time by the committing court, or by the parent, division, county department or certified organization transferring such custody. (d) The term "division" shall mean the division of social administration of the department of public welfare of the state of Ohio and any unit or bureau thereof. (e) The term "county department" shall mean a county welfare department which has assumed the administration of child welfare. The term "board" shall mean a county child welfare board. (f) The singular number shall include the plural, and the masculine gender shall include the feminine.1

DISCUSSION: 

The "adoption code" was enacted by the 95th General

Assembly in 1943 on the recommendation of the Children's Code Commission appointed by the Governor, after an extensive study in which various welfare organizations including the State Association of Juvenile 

Court Judges of Ohio participated. The new code seek to insure that no child shall be adopted by improper persons. 

Adoption is the legal authorization given to one to take another's child into his own family, thereby giving to such child all the rights, privileges and duties of a natural child and heir.3

SEC. 10512-10 G. C. WHO MAY PETITION FOR LEAVE TO ADOPT A CHILD. 

A husband and wife jointly or a step-parent married to one of the natural or legal parents of the child sought to be adopted, or any other proper person, may petition the probate court of the county in which the petitioner resides or of the county in which the child was born or has a legal settlement or residence or has become a public charge, for leave to adopt a child and for a change of the name of such child.4 

DISCUSSION: 

Similar sections have been held constitutional,5 

Petitions for adoption must be filed in the probate court. It may be filed in the county where the petitioner 

1. Sec. 10512-9, G. C., 121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 

14 Am. St. Rep. 500. 3. 

4. Sec. 10512-10, G. C., 120 O. L., 434, effective Jan. 1, 1944. 5. 

In re Todhunter, 33 O. L. A., 567, 35 N. E. (2d) 992. 302

or petitioners reside, or in the county where the child was born or has a legal settlement or residence or in which the child was born or has become a public charge, or in the county where the child had a legal residence before it became a public charge. 

A legal settlement means a residence which would entitle a person to public support or assistance when in need of relief. To acquire a residence, a person must reside in the state one year, in a county one year, and in a city or township within a county three months without relief.6 

A child has the same domicile as its father if alive, or if the father is dead, that of the mother during widowhood, or if given custody in divorce. A child living with a stepfather, and whose father is dead, has the domicile of the stepfather if the mother is living with the stepfather.7

A minor has the same domicile with whom it stands in the relation of "loco parentis."8

Where proceedings are had in a court without jurisdiction, such proceedings are not void but voidable.9

There is a presumption that all provisions of law were legally complied with.10 A defective proceeding cannot be collaterally attacked if the parties have borne the defacto relation of parent and child for a long time.11 

A single person may adopt a child. A court cannot authorize the adoption of a child to a married person unless the spouse joins in the application.12 A husband and wife should be in accord, because it is provided that the court must be satisfied that both desire it from the free will and accord of each. 13

Generally the court should not permit aged people to adopt a child. There should be a reasonable expectancy that the adopting parents will live during the minority of the child, unless such persons have sufficient means to provide for its support after death. 

The child must be a minor.1 Any relative may be adopted, and a stepchild must be adopted to entitle a child to all the rights and privileges of a child of the step-parent.2

An oral contract for adoption whereby the adopting parents agree to devise and bequeath both real and personal property to the adopted child is unenforceable by 6. 7. 

Sec. 3477, G. C.; Sec. 3479, G. C. 

Commissioners vs. Commissioners, 116 O. S., 663, 158 N. E. 172. 

49 L. R. A. (N. S.), 875. 8. 9. 

McClain vs. Lyon, 24 Ohio App., 279. 10. 

Martin vs. Fisher, 25 Ohio App., 372, 5 O. L. A., 596. 

11. Taylor vs. Bushnell, 29 O. C. A., 497, 35 O. C. D., 642. 12. 

Blaustein vs. Blaustein, 77 Ohio App., 281, 66 N. E. (2d) 156. 

Sec. 10512-18, G. C. 13. 1. 

Steele vs. Swartz, 55 O. S., 685, 48 N. E. 1118. 

2. Kraft vs. Wolfe, 3 O. N. P. (N. S.), 105, O. D., 554. 303

reason of its failure to comply with the statutes of frauds, (G. C. Sec. 8621), and the performance of such oral contract by giving up the custody of such child to its adopting parents is not such part performance as to take the case out of the statute.3

A written contract to adopt is valid and will be enforced where the contract regarding adoption has been fully performed except the adoption,4 but a contract to adopt which covered apprenticeship also, is inconsistent, and a child cannot claim heirship rights under it if the property rights were not settled by the contract.5 

SEC. 10512-11 G. C. 

PETITION FOR ADOPTION. 

The petition for adoption shall be verified by each peti tioner. It shall contain: 

(a) The name, date and place of birth, and place of residence of each petitioner; (b) The name, date and place of birth, and place of residence of the child sought to be adopted; (c) The relationship, if any, of the child to the petitioner; (d) The full name by which the child shall be known after adoption, which name shall be the only name appearing in the caption of the petition for adoption; (e) A full description of the property, if any, of the child; (f) The names of the parents of the child, and the address of each living parent; provided that if the child is in the permanent custody of the division, a county department, or a certified organization, the names and addresses of parents may be omitted; (g) The name and address of the legal guardian of the person of the child if any has been appointed; (h) Any further facts necessary for a determination of the person or persons whose consent to the adoption is required to be obtained pursuant to section 10512-14; (i) If the child is living in the home of the petitioner, the name of the person, county, department, board, organization or division who placed the child in such home and the date of placement; when the child enters the home of the petitioner after the filing of the petition such information respecting the placement shall forthwith be furnished to the court by a supplemental petition.

A certified copy of the child's birth certificate shall be filed with the petition.6 5. 6. 7.

DISCUSSION: 

The word "person" as used in paragraph (i) of this section means a "human being."7

Shuttleworth, 5 Ohio App., 137, 27 O. C. D., 234. 

Sissons vs. Irish, 23 Ohio App., 462, 155 N. E. 168. 

Sec. 10512-11, G. C., 121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946.

Blank forms of petitions and consents can be obtained in all probate court offices. 

SEC. 10512-12 G. C. TIME OF HEARING; APPOINTMENT OF NEXT FRIEND. 

Upon the filing of the petition, or, if the child entered the home of the petitioner after the filing of the petition, then upon filing of such supplemental petition, the court shall fix a day for hearing which shall be not less than thirty nor more than sixty days after the filing of the petition or such supplemental petition, shall appoint a next friend to the child, and shall cause notice to be given to the guardian of the person of such child, if any, and to the parents or parent of the child if the parents' consent is required as provided in section. 

10501-21 of the General Code.8

DISCUSSION: 

Sec. 10216 G. C. provides that in fixing the time an act is required to be done, the first day shall be excluded and the last day included, except that the last day shall be excluded if it be Sunday.

SEC. 10512-13 G. C. QUALIFICATIONS OF NEXT FRIEND; 

INVESTIGATION AND REPORT OF NEXT FRIEND. 

The next friend shall be a county department, a county child welfare board, or certified organization or the division, or some person qualified by training and experience to perform the functions hereinafter described. If the child sought to be adopted is in the permanent custody of the division, a county department, a county child welfare board, a certified organization the division, county, department, board or organization shall be appointed as the next friend. The next friend so appointed shall proceed to make a thorough investigation into the suitability of the adoption. Such investigation shall include, in addition to any other information the court may require in the particular case, inquiries as to: (1) The physical and mental health, emotional stability and personal integrity of the petitioner and the ability of the petitioner to promote the welfare of the child; (2) The physical and mental condition of the child; (3) The child's family background, including names and identifying data regarding the parents; (4) Reasons for the child's placement away from his parents, their attitude toward the proposed adoption, and the circumstances under which the child came into the home of the petitioner; (5) The suitability of the adoption of this child by this petitioner, taking into account their respective racial, religious and cultural backgrounds, and the child's own attitude toward the adoption in any case in which the child's age makes this feasible. 

At least three days prior to the date set for hearing, the next friend shall submit to the court a full report in writing on a form prescribed by the division, with a written statement approving or disapproving the proposed adoption and any other information concerning such child or the proposed home which the court may require. 

The reports of the investigation required by subsections 2, 3 and 4 hereof shall not be filed with the balance of the 306

adoption proceedings, but shall be filed separately and shall be available for inspection only upon the personal direction of the Probate Judge;

The cost of the proceedings shall be paid by the petitioner, and the court may include in such costs a reasonable sum for the services and the necessary expenses of the next friend which is to be paid to such next friend; except that if such next friend be a public employee, such part of the costs as is represented by his services and expenses shall be distributed and paid into the county treasury as a part of the general costs.9

DISCUSSION: 

No petition for adoption can be heard until after an examination of the home of the petitioners, and an investigation as required by this section, has been made by a party appointed by the court. A next friend cannot be appointed by Nunc Pro Tunc order if another was previously appointed.10

SEC. 10512-14 G. C. WRITTEN CONSENT REQUIRED. 

No final decree or interlocutory order of adoption shall be entered by the court unless there shall be filed with the court. written consents to the adoption, verified or acknowledged, 

(1) By the child if over twelve years of age, provided however, that if such child over twelve years of age has resided in the home of the petitioner continuously for a period of eight years, or more, immediately preceding the filing of the petition for adoption, the written consent of such child shall not be required; (2) By each of the living parents, adult or minor, except as follows: (a) The mother of an illegitimate child shall be considered for purposes of this section to be the sole parent and may give such consent alone, in which case the consent shall state that it is given by the mother by virtue of the fact that she is the sole parent; provided however, that if a mother is a married woman, notice of the adoption proceedings shall be given in such manner as the court may direct, to her husband whose consent shall not be necessary to the adoption unless he is the natural father of such child; provided further, that if the father is a married man, notice of the adoption proceedings shall be given in such manner as the court may direct to his wife, whose consent shall not be necessary to the adoption unless she is the natural mother of such child; if such mother is physically unable to appear in open court to execute such consent, she may execute such consent in the presence of the next friend; 

9. Sec. 10512-13, G. C., 121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 

10. In re Privette, 45 Ohio App., 51, 185 N. E. 435. 307

(b) The consent of a parent shall not be required if the child is in the permanent custody of the division, a county department, a county child welfare board, or a certified organization; (c) If a parent has been adjudged incompetent by reason of mental disability, the probate court shall appoint a guardian ad litem who shall investigate the situation surrounding the parent and the child, and if satisfied that the adoption should be completed, execute the consent. The court may allow such guardian ad litem a reasonable fee for his services which shall be taxed as a part of the costs of the proceeding to be paid by the petitioner. 

The written consent to the adoption, herein provided for, shall not be filed with the balance of the adoption proceedings, but shall be filed separately and shall be available for inspection only upon the personal direction of the Probate Judge; (d) If it is alleged in the petition that one or both of the parents have wilfully failed to properly support and maintain the child for a period of more than two years immediately preceding the filing of the petition, the court shall cause notice of the filing of said petition and the allegations of such failure to be given such parent or parents as provided in section 10501-21 of the General Code. After such notice has been given, the probate court shall determine the issue as to such failure to properly support and maintain the child. The consent of the parent found by the probate court to have wilfully failed to properly support and maintain the child for such period shall not be required. (e) If the parents are dead or their place of residence is unknown and cannot with reasonable diligence be ascertained or there is no guardian of the person of such child, the consent may be given by the next friend. (3) If a guardian of the person of the child has been appointed and is acting as such, then by such guardian. (4) If the child is in the permanent custody of the division, a county department, a county child welfare board, or a certified organization, then by the division or such department, board or organization. This provision shall apply to out-ofstate custodians the same as to those within the state. 

Such consents shall be applicable only to the specific adoption proposed by the petition. Such consents may not be withdrawn after the entry of an interlocutory order, or after the final decree, of adoption.

No final decree or interlocutory order of adoption shall be entered with respect to any child in the custody of the juvenile court or concerning whose custody or disposition proceedings are pending in such court until such custody or proceedings have been suspended or terminated by or in such court.11 

11. Sec. 10512-14, G. C., 121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 308

DISCUSSION: 

Where there is requirement that a consent must be given, witnessed and acknowledged, it is mandatory and must be strictly followed.1

The provision that a consent could be given by the parent awarded custody in a divorce proceeding if approved by the court granting divorce was valid,2 but such approval is no longer required.

A person who has given a consent may withdraw it at any time before the initial hearing.3

A consent under a decree of a juvenile court finding a child to be dependent is invalid if the necessary notice was not given in the proceeding to declare the child a dependent.4

A parent who is a minor may give consent.5 

The fact that the parents of an illegitimate child have consented to its adoption in an adoption proceeding in the probate court does not bar the child's father from prosecuting an action in habeas corpus for its custody against the one to whom its custody was surrendered.6 

A ward of the juvenile court may be adopted regardless of Sec. 1639-37 G. C.7

The consent of a parent who has been awarded custody in a divorce decree is sufficient without court approval if the other parent "has neglected or refused" to support the child for two years.8

Notice of adoption proceedings must now be given to the husband of a married woman who gives consent to the adoption of her child even though the husband is not the father of such child. Likewise, a notice must be given to the wife of a husband who gives consent to the adoption of his child even though the wife is not the mother of such child.

It is reversible error for a court to render a decree of adoption without the mother's consent where it is alleged that she "has not supported" it for two years but no allegation or proof offered that she "has failed or refused" to do so.9

A proceeding instituted by a husband and wife to adopt the wife's son by a former divorced husband is an action in which such former divorced husband is involved, with- 3.

in the meaning of the Soldiers and Sailors Relief Act of 1940.10 

A child under two years of age may be placed with any certified association or institution permanently for adoption without the consent of the state division of social administration or the juvenile court, but a child may not be placed in the custody of a non-relative, or unlicensed association or institution either temporary or permanently without the written consent of the state division of social administration. A child may be placed temporarily with relatives or in a legally licensed boarding home without such consent.11

SEC. 10512-16 G. C. PLACEMENT OF CHILD IN VIOLA- TION OF LAW; JURISDICTION OF JUVENILE COURT. 

If the probate court finds that a child sought to be adopted was placed in the home of the petitioner in violation of the laws relating to the placement of children in foster homes, it may certify a copy of such finding to the juvenile court of the county where the child is living with the petitioner and shall suspend further action on the petition. Thereupon the juvenile court, after notice to the parents of the child, the petitioners, the persons who placed the child in said home, and all other persons in interest as provided in section 10501-21 of the General Code, and hearing, shall determine whether such placement is for the best interest of the child, shall either approve or disapprove such placement and shall certify a copy of its findings to the probate court wherein the petition is filed. If the placement is disapproved the juvenile court shall retain jurisdiction, shall order the child removed from the home of the petitioner and shall determine the custody and disposition of the child. If such finding is not certified to the juvenile court, the probate court, after notice to the persons heretofore set out as provided in section 10501-21 of the General Code and hearing, shall approve or disapprove such placement. If placement is disapproved by the probate court, a certified copy of such finding shall be certified to the juvenile court of the county where the child is living with the petitioner, for appropriate action in accord with this section. 

121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946.

SEC. 10512-17 G. C. CONSENT WHERE CHILD PREVI- OUSLY ADOPTED; INHERITANCE RIGHTS OF ADOPTED CHILD.. 

In proceedings for the adoption of a child previously adoped, the written consent of the preceding adopting parent or parents shall be required in the same manner and to the same extent as the written consent of the natural parent or parents in a first adoption is required. In the event an adopted child is subsequently adopted by its natural parent or parents, its status as a natural child of such parent or parents shall be restored as fully as though such child had never been adopted by anyone else.1

SEC. 10512-18 G. C. 

HEARING; EXAMINATION 

OF 

CHILD; EXAMINATION OF  SEPARATELY; INTERLOCUTORY DECREE. 

Upon the date set, the court shall proceed to a full hearing on the petition, shall examine under oath the petitioner, the child if over twelve years of age and its consent is required, the next friend and all other persons in interest who are present and to whom lawful notice has been given; and may 

14. Sec. 10512-16, G. C., 121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 

1. Sec. 10512-17, G. C., 121 O. L. S., 186, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 312

also examine any other person having information or knowledge pertinent to the adoption. When the petition is filed by a husband and wife, the court shall examine each separate and apart from the other and refuse leave of such adoption unless satisfied from the examination that each petitioner of his or her own free will and accord desires it. The court may adjourn the hearing from time to time. 

If after such hearing the court is satisfied that the requirements of this adoption code have been complied with, that the petitioner is suitably qualified to care for and rear the child and that the best interests of the child will be promoted by the adoption, it shall enter an interlocutory order of adoption declaring that henceforth, subject to the final decree of the court, the child shall have the status of the adopted child of the petitioner; provided that property rights shall not be affected by such interlocutory order. 

Provided that the court may dispense with the probationary period provided for in such interlocutory order and may, after such hearing, enter a final decree of adoption: (a) If the child is legally the child by birth or adoption of the spouse of the petitioner and is living in the home of the petitioner and his spouse, or (b) If the child has been placed in the home of the petitioner by the division, a county department, a board or a certified organization, has lived in the home of the petitioner continuously for the six months next preceding the date of the hearing, has been visited by a representative of the division, the county department, a board or organization at reasonable intervals during such period, and the division or the county department, board or organization recommends the adoption, or (c) If the child was legally placed in the home of the petitioner, or its placement therein has been found by the proper court to be beneficial to the child and has resided therein continuously for a period of six months.

The granting of an interlocutory order, or a final decree where the probationary period is dispensed with, shall terminate the jurisdiction of a divorce court over such child. A certified copy of the order of decree shall be mailed or delivered to the court then having jurisdiction of the action for divorce.2

The Court further finds that all of the requirements of the adoption code have been complied with; that the petitioner.. qualified

SEC. 10512-19 G. C. VISITS AND REPORT BY NEXT FRIEND. 

After the entry of an interlocutory order of adoption the next friend shall visit the child in the home of the petitioner at reasonable intervals and within the sixth month following the interlocutory order shall submit to the court a further written report in a form prescribed by the division of his findings relative to the suitability of the adoption. Provided that if the petitioners are residing or move during such period outside the county and such visitation by the next friend is impracticable the court may arrange for such visitation and report by or through the division.3

DISCUSSION: 

Form for visitation report is not given due to the fact that the State Welfare Department must prescribe such form.

SEC. 10512-20 G. C. REVOCATION OF INTERLOCUTORY DECREE; FINAL DECREE; CERTIFICATION TO STATE DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH. 

Upon a hearing, after notice by registered mail or otherwise, as the court may direct or approve, to the petitioner and the next friend and to any other person, organization or public agency which has consented to the adoption, the court may, by order duly entered, revoke its interlocutory order of adoption at any time prior to the entry of the final decree, if it finds that the adoption will not be in the best interest of the child or for any good cause shown. Such action may be taken by the court on its own motion, or on motion of any interested party.

Upon application of the petitioner and the payment of costs the interlocutory order shall be revoked and the petition dismissed at any time prior to the entry of the final decree. 

After the expiration of six months from the date upon which the interlocutory order is entered if such order has not been revoked, the court shall enter a final decree of adoption, unless the court finds that it would be to the best interests of the child to extend the period of the interlocutory order. 

Upon entry of the final decree the court shall forward to the

SEC. 10512-21 G. C. DISPOSITION OF CHILD; PETITION DISMISSED. 

If for any reason whatsoever the petition shall be dismissed or the court shall deny or revoke its interlocutory order of adoption or deny a final decree of adoption, the following disposition shall be made of the child: (a) If the child is, or was prior to the interlocutory order, in the permanent custody of the division or a county department or certified organization, then the child shall be returned to the division or such department or organization. (b) Otherwise the cause shall be certified to the juvenile court of the county where the child is then residing for appropriate action and disposition by such court.5 

ORDER ON DENIAL OF INTERLOCUTORY DECREE OR FINAL DECREE Title.

SEC. 10512-22 G. C. RECORD OF PETITION, LOCUTORY ORDER, DECREE, ETC. 

INTER- The petition, the interlocutory order, the final decree of adoption and proceedings shall be recorded in a book kept for such purposes and separately indexed; such book shall be a part of the records of the probate court (,) and all consents, affidavits and other papers shall be properly filed. Such papers, records and books shall not be open to inspection or copy by a person other than the parties of record and their attorneys, except upon order of the court for good cause shown.6

DISCUSSION: 

A record of adoption imports absolute verity in a collateral proceeding and it will be presumed that the provisions of law have been complied with.7

SEC. 10512-23 G. C. LEGAL RIGHTS, ETC.; OF ADOPT- ING PARENT AND ADOPTED CHILD. 

Except as hereinafter provided in the case of adoption by a step-father or step-mother, the natural parents, if living, shall be divested of all legal rights and obligations due from them to the child or from the child to them, and the child shall be free from all legal obligations of obedience or otherwise to such parents; and the adopting parent or parents of the child shall be invested with every legal right in respect to obedience and maintenance on the part of the child as if said child had been born to them in lawful wedlock; and the child shall be invested with every legal right, privilege, obligation and relation in respect to education, maintenance and the rights of inheritance to real estate, or to the distribution of personal estate on the death of such adopting parent or parents as if born to them in lawful wedlock; provided si h child shall not be capable of inheriting property expressly limited to heirs of the body of the adopting parent or parents; but shall be capable of inheriting property expressly limited by will or by operation of law to the child or children, heir or heirs at law, or next of kin, of the adopting parent or parents, or to a class including any of the foregoing and provided, also, if such adopting parent or parents shall have other child or children, then the children by birth and adoption shall, respectively, inherit from and through each other as if all had been children of the same parents born in lawful wedlock. 

Nothing in this act shall be construed as debarring a legally 

6. Sec. 10512-22, G. C., 120 O. L., 434, effective Jan. 1, 1944. 7. 

Lawson vs. Thomas, 48 Ohio App., 311, 193 N. E., 655. 316

adopted child from inheriting property of its natural parents or other kin. Provided, however, that in case of adoptions by a step-father or step-mother the rights and obligations of the natural parent who is the spouse of the adopting stepparent shall not in any way be affected by such decree.8 

DISCUSSION: 

Adopting parents are entitled to the custody of an adopted child, to the exclusion of all others, unless circumstances require a change in custody.9 It was formerly held that an adopted child could inherit from its adopted parents, but not through them from their ancestors. But under this section, an adopted child inherits through as well as from the adopting parents, whether the property passes by will or operation of law.10 The descendants of an adopted child inherit the same as grandchildren of the blood.1 

The word "issue,"2 "child or other relative," and "heirs at law,"4 include an adopted child, but it is not included in "heirs of the body."

While it was formerly held that natural parents and not foster parents inherit from an adopted child upon the death of such child intestate and without a spouse or lineal heirs,6 it is now probable that the adopting parents as well as the natural parents both inherit in such a case.7

The adoption of a child after the execution of a will has the same effect as the birth of a child.5 

For all other purposes, an adopted child is to be considered the same as a natural child.8 It includes the right of either parent or child to recover through the legal representative for the wrongful death of either.9 

But an adopted child cannot take under a trust deed antedating adoption if such right was not authorized by the statute in force at the time the trust deed was executed.1 2. 3. 4. 5. 

7. 8. 

Sec. 10512-23 G. C., 120 O. L., 434, effective Jan. 1, 1944. 

Martin vs. Fisher, 25 Ohio App., 372, 158 N. E. 287. 9. 10. 

Flynn vs. Brodbeck, 147 O. S., 49, 68 N. E. (2d) 75.

Miller vs. Shepard, 29 Ohio App., 22, 162, N. E. 788. 

White vs. Agnew, 38 Bull. 74, 56 O. S., 796. 

Smith vs. Hunter, 86 O. S., 106, 99 N. E. 91.

Upon the death of the adoptive parents of an adopted minor child, no person including the daughter of the adopted parents is entitled to custody as a matter of right. A court will consider the best interests of the

child in awarding custody under such circumstances.2

Under the laws of a sister state which require a husband and wife to join in a petition for adoption, an order of adoption based on a petition of the husband only is void.3

SEC. 11181 G. C. WHO MAY CONTRACT MATRIMONY. 

Male persons of the age of eighteen years, and female persons of the age of sixteen years, not nearer of kin than second cousins, and not having a husband or wife living, may be joined in marriage. Any such minor must first obtain the consent of his or her parents, surviving parent, parent awarded custody by a court of competent jurisdiction, the guardian of his or her person, or any one of the following who has been awarded permanent custody of such minor by a court exercising juvenile jurisdiction; an adult person, the state department of public welfare or any child welfare organization certified by such department, a county department of welfare or a county child welfare board. 

Provided, however, that any such minor shall not be required to obtain the consent of a parent who resides in a foreign country, has neglected or abandoned such minor for a period of one year or longer immediately preceding such minor's application for a marriage license, has been adjudged incompetent, is an inmate of a state mental or penal institution, has been permanently deprived of custody of such minor by a court exercising juvenile jurisdiction or has been deprived of the custody or control or both of such minor by the appointment of a guardian of the person of the minor by the probate court.1 

DISCUSSION: 

Long continued cohabitation as man and wife and general recognition in the community and by the husband's niece as such, added to numerous statements to friends of a ceremonial marriage in Arizona in 1915, and testimony of one that he saw and read the marriage contract, coupled with the possibility that the marriage could have been performed in Arizona without being recorded, all together makes out a marriage between the parties. 

Where a claim is made that two persons were married in another state and that state does not recognize common law marriages, the marriage, under the rule that lex loci controls marriage contracts, must be based on proof of a ceremonial marriage or none at all.2

One who claims the right to inherit as an heir through the alleged fact that his mother was the common-law wife of decedent, does not establish his right where there 

2. In re Griffin, 30 O. O., 367, 15 Ohio Supp. 101. 3. 

Blaustein vs. Blaustein, 77 Ohio App., 281, 66 N. E. (2d) 156. 

Sec. 11181, G. C., 121 O. L. H., 463, effective Jan. 1, 1946. 1. 

2. Rea vs. Fornan, 37 O. L. A., 135, 46 N. E. (2d) 649. 318

is insufficient proof of the holding out of the husbandwife relationship that it may be said that she was reputed in the community and circle in which they moved to be his wife.3

There is a presumption that any marriage once solemnized is valid and continues until terminated by death or decree of court where both are before it by due process of law. The parties claiming the validity of a second marriage have the burden of overcoming this presumption but such rule is not applicable where there is no credible evidence of a claimed first marriage.4

The fact that a man and woman have lived together with every intimate association consistent with the life of persons legally married does not constitute a commonlaw marriage where there was never an agreement or contract of any kind.5

A marriage entered into by proxy in Mexico in conformity with the laws of Mexico where such a marriage is valid, at a time when one of the parties was in Ohio and the other in England, will be recognized as a valid marriage in Ohio. Cohabitation is not necessary to a valid marriage status in Ohio.6 

An agreement to marry in praesenti, made by parties competent to contract, accompanied and followed by cohabitation, as husband and wife, with the result that they are treated and reputed as husband and wife in the community in which they reside, constitutes a commonlaw marriage. While such agreement to marry in praesenti must be proved by clear and convincing evidence, it may be established by proof of the acts, declarations and conduct of the parties and their recognized status in the community in which they reside.7 

SEC. 11181-1 G. C. CONSENT OF JUVENILE JUDGE.

Markley vs. Hudson, 143 O. S., 163, 54 N. E. (2d) 304; In re Redman, 135 O. S., 554, 14 O. O., 426. 319

made wards of the court and it is found proper, said court may, with consent of said wards, or their parents, if any are living, or of any guardian or custodian give consent in the probate court, and the probate court may thereupon issue a license therefor, notwithstanding either or both the contracting parties for the marital relation may be under the minimum age otherwise prescribed by law, but such license shall not issue until the provisions of section 11188 have been complied with and until such illegitimate child has been born or until it is found beyond doubt it is a maternity case.8

Chapter XXIII DIVORCE AND ALIMONY SECTION 11979. For What Cause Divorce May Be Granted. 

Divorce Not to Affect Legitimacy of Children. 

11997. Cause For Which Alimony Allowed. 11987. 

SEC. 11979 G. C. FOR WHAT CAUSE DIVORCE MAY BE GRANTED. 

Courts of common pleas may grant divorces for the following causes: 

1. That either party had a husband or wife living at the time of the marriage from which the divorce is sought; 

2. Wilful absence of either party from the other for three years; 

3. Adultery; 

4. Impotency; 

5. Extreme cruelty; 

6. Fraudulent contract; 

7. Any gross neglect of duty; 

8. Habitual drunkenness for three years; 

9. The imprisonment of either party in a penitentiary under sentence thereto. The petition for divorce under this clause must be filed during the imprisonment of the adverse party; 

10. The procurement of a divorce without this state, by a husband or wife, by virtue of which the party who procured it is released from the obligations of the marriage, while they remain binding upon the other party.1

DISCUSSION: 

Under the provisions of pargraph (c) of Section 163916 of the General Code, all the juvenile courts of the state, except the Cuyahoga County Juvenile Court, have jurisdiction to hear, determine and make a record of any action for divorce and alimony involving the custody or care of children certified to a juvenile court by the court of common pleas. Three sections of the Divorce and Alimony Chapter of the General Code are included in this work for reference purposes only. Juvenile Courts hearing divorce and alimony cases are referred to Lamneck's "Ohio Divorce and Alimony" for a full discussion of the law and procedure on this subject. 

For form for certification, see form under Section 1639-16 G. C. 

1. Sec. 11979, G. C., R. S. 5689. 321

DIVORCE AND ALIMONY 

SEC. 11987 G. C. DIVORCE NOT TO AFFECT LEGITI- MACY OF CHILDREN. 

The granting of a divorce and dissolution of the marriage in nowise shall affect the legitimacy of children of the parties thereto. The court shall make such order for the disposition, care and maintenance of the children, if any, as is just. Provided that if the court finds with respect to any child under eighteen years of age that neither parent is a suitable person to have the custody of such child, the court shall, with the consent of the juvenile court, certify a copy of its findings, together with so much of the record and such further information in narrative form or otherwise, as the court deems necessary or the juvenile court may request, to the juvenile court for further proceedings according to law, and thereupon the juvenile court shall have exclusive jurisdiction respecting such child.2

DISCUSSION: 

Under the provisions of Sections 1639-16 and 8034-1 

of the General Code, the juvenile court has jurisdiction to hear and determine the care, custody or support of any child certified to the juvenile court by another court having jurisdiction over such child for further proceedings according to law.

Before the court of common pleas can certify a case of children to the juvenile court, the consent of the juvenile judge must be first obtained.3

When the court of common pleas surrenders jurisdiction over a child to the juvenile court, it loses its jurisdiction and the juvenile court has continuing and exclusive jurisdiction thereafter.4 

See Section 1639-16 for form certifying jurisdiction of the care, custody and support of a child to the Juvenile Court.

Such petition or cross-petition for alimony may be for the following causes: 

1. Adultery; 

2. Any gross neglect of duty; 

3. Abandonment of the wife without good cause;

SEC. 12110 G. C. COMPLAINT OF UNMARRIED WOMAN. 

When an unmarried woman, who has been delivered of or is pregnant with a bastard child, makes a complaint in writing, under oath, before a justice of the peace, or in the juvenile court charging a person with being the father of such child, the judge or justice thereupon shall issue his warrant, direct- 325

BASTARDY 

ed to any sheriff, police officer or constable of the state, commanding him to pursue and arrest such accused person in any county therein, and bring him forthwith before such judge or justice to answer such complaint.1

DISCUSSION: 

An action in bastardy though criminal in form, is a civil action, and the relief sought and the end to be attained are clearly of a civil nature.2 A defendant, if acquitted, cannot plead former jeopardy if a new trial is granted3 and only three-fourths or more of a jury is required to render a verdict.4 The suit should be carried on in the name of the party complaining and the prosecuting attorney is not bound to attend.5 1. 2. 3. 4. (a) Jurisdiction. An affidavit may be filed in any county of Ohio, even though the complainant is not a resident of the county and the child was not born in Ohio.6 This applies even though the person alleged to be the father is a resident of another county.7 10. 11. 

(b) When Action Lies. A married woman cannot main tain an action, unless the prosecution was begun before marriage,8 neither can proceedings be maintained if the child was born before marriage.9

When a child was begotten during marriage, but born after a divorce, and it was shown that the husband had deserted and had no contact with the mother for several years, a suit against a third person can be maintained.10 

But a child begotten during lawful wedlock is presumed to be legitimate and will not be adjudged illegitimate unless it can be shown that it was impossible for the husband to be the father.11

Thus a woman who is unmarried (not having a lawful husband) and pregnant, may institute a bastardy proceeding charging one other than her former husband with being the father of the child, even though such child was conceived during the existence of a lawful marriage.1 

Marriage is no defense if the marriage is void.2

A dead foetus or embryo child is not a child upon which

an action can be based3, but an action does not abate insofar as it relates to support, maintenance and necessary expenses caused by pregnancy if the complaint is filed before the dead foetus is expelled from the mother's womb.4 (c) Costs. A non-resident complainant can be required to secure the costs.5 (d) Complaint. The complaint should show that the complainant is unmarried,6 that the complainant is pregnant or has been delivered of a bastard child and that the defendant is the father of the child. A minor may institute the proceeding without the intervention of a next friend and no guardian ad litem is required for a minor defendant.7 

A complaint may be sworn to before a notary public.8 (f) Appeal. A bastardy proceeding, while having some characteristics of criminal prosecution, is essentially civil action and appeal from judgment therein is not controlled by provisions of Section 1639-51, General Code, relating to appeals from judgments in criminal prosecution of adults in Juvenile Court.9

SEC. 12111 G. C. EXAMINATION. 

On the return of the warrant, the justice or judge shall examine the complainant, under oath, in the presence of the 328

accused, respecting the cause of her complaint. The accused shall be permitted also to ask her, when under oath, any question he may think necessary for his defense.10

DISCUSSION: 

The complainant is the only witness that can be examined at the preliminary hearing.11

The statutory provisions relative to the preliminary examination of a complainant may be waived by the accused. The provision of Sec. 12122 G. C., that "the examination before the justice shall be given in evidence by the complainant," is directory only, and does not apply where the case originated in the Juvenile Court.12 VS.

SEC. 12112 G. C. REDUCE TO WRITING. 

The examination of complainant by the judge or justice, the questions of the defendant, and the answers thereto by her must be reduced to writing, in the presence of the judge or justice, and subscribed by her.13

DISCUSSION: 

The failure of the complainant to subscribe to the examination does not render the proceeding void.1

SEC. 12113 G. C. ADJOURNMENT OF HEARING; BOND. 

On the request of either party, for good cause shown, the judge or justice may continue the examination for a period not to exceed ten days, upon the accused entering into a recognizance to the state, with sufficient surety, in not less than three hundred nor more than one thousand dollars, to appear and answer the complaint, at the time fixed for its hearing, and abide the order of the judge or justice.2

SEC. 12114 G. C. 

If, during the examination before the justice or judge, or before judgment in the court of common pleas, or juvenile court, the accused pays or secures to be paid to the complainant such amount of money or property as she agrees to receive in full satisfaction of all claims she may have individually against said accused arising out of said complaint, such justice, court, or judge, shall discharge him from custody, upon his paying the costs of prosecution. Such agreement must be made or acknowledged by both parties in the presence of the justice, court, or judge, who thereupon shall enter a memorandum thereof on his docket or cause it to be made upon his journal. Provided, however, that nothing in this section shall be construed as a bar to the prosecution of the accused for failure to support his illegitimate child or children under the provisions of any statute providing for prosecution and punishment for the non-support of legitimate or illegitimate children.3

of the peace, the justice before whom the complaint was made shall bind the accused to appear before the juvenile court or at the next term of the common pleas court, in a recognizance to the state, with sufficient surety, in not less than three hundred nor more than one thousand dollars, to answer the accusation, and abide the order of the court. On neglect or refusal to find such security, the justice or judge shall cause the accused to be committed to the jail of the county, there to be held to answer the complaint.5

SEC. 12116 G. C. RECOGNIZANCE FOR DISCHARGE. 

A person committed to jail for failure to give such recognizance may be discharged from custody by entering into recognizance, with sufficient surety, in not less than three hundred nor more than one thousand dollars, to be taken and approved by a judge of the court in which said cause is pending.8

SEC. 12117 G. C. JUSTICE SHALL FILE TRANSCRIPT AND PAPERS WITH CLERK. 

8. Sec. 12116, G. C., 110 O. L., 298. 

9. Sec. 12117, G. C., R. S. 5620. 10. 

Hoff vs. Fisher, 26 O. S., 7. 

1. Sec. 12118, G. C., 110 O. L., 298. 

Porter vs. State, 23 O. S., 320. 2. 

3. Grieve vs. Freytag, 31 O. S., 147.

The justice before whom the examination is had, within thirty days thereafter, shall file with the clerk of the common pleas court of the county a certified transcript of the proceedings, together with the recognizance, if any be taken, and all other papers therein.9

DISCUSSION: 

Because the transcript is not filed within the required time does not defeat the action. If the transcript or written examination has been lost, it may be proven by parole. The court may or may not continue a case until lost papers are found.10

SEC. 12118 G. C. 

CONTINUANCE. 

If, on the day fixed for trial, the complainant has not been delivered, or is unable to attend, or if there be any other sufficient reason therefor, the court may order a continuance of the cause. Such continuance shall operate as a renewal of the recognizance, which shall remain in full force until final judgment.1 

DISCUSSION: 

A bastardy case may be continued from time to time,2 but a trial had at a subsequent third term without a continuance is unauthorized.3 334

SEC. 12119 G. C. SURRENDER OF ACCUSED BY SURE- TIES. 

At any term of the court of common pleas, or at any time before trial in the juvenile court, if the sureties on the recognizance surrender the accused and request to be released therefrom, or if the court deems the recognizance insufficient, it may order a new recognizance to be taken, cancel the first, and commit the accused until a new recognizance is given.4 

SEC. 12120 G. C. FORFEITURE OF BOND. 

If the accused fails to appear at the term of court to which he is recognized, or at the time set for trial in the juvenile court, his recognizance shall be forfeited. If a verdict of guilty be rendered, and the judgment entered thereon as hereinafter provided, the amount of such forfeited recognizance shall be applied in payment of the judgment.5

SEC. 12121 G. C. ACCUSED TO BE PERMITTED TO DE- FEND. 

Before or on the hearing of the complaint, the court shall permit the accused to appear in person, or by counsel, and make defense.6

SEC. 12122 G. C. THE TRIAL IN COURT. 

When, before the court to which he is recognized to appear, the accused pleads not guilty of the charge, or, having been recognized, fails to appear, the court shall order the issue to be tried by a jury. At the trial, the examination before the justice shall be given in evidence by the complainant.7 

DISCUSSION: 

(a) Trial. The same jury may be used for the aring of several bastardy cases, provided the cases are so far advanced as to enable the court to determine the necessity of a jury in each case.8

A trial cannot proceed without the presence of the complainant,9 and she is a proper witness and can testify who the father of the child is.10 

Failure to read the transcript of evidence taken at the preliminary hearing, before the complainant rests, is not prejudicial error where the reading occurred before the defendant was placed on the witness stand,11 or where the case originated in the juvenile court, and the

8. 10. defendant did not require the preliminary examination to be read.1 (b) Evidence. An unsigned preliminary examination may be admitted in evidence, if the trial begins without objection on the part of the defendant.2 

The fact that the complainant associated with another man between six months and a year before conception is too remote and is immaterial.3

The child may be exhibited to the jury to show whether it resembles the defendant,4 and where a child two years old is exhibited to the jury, a judgment on the weight the evidence will not be reversed.5

A witness who has denied the authenticity of a letter material to the issue may be required, on cross examination, to subject specimens of her writing for comparison but this cannot be done on direct examination.6 

It is not conclusive that one charged with being the father of a bastard child is not guilty when the period of gestation was only 259 days.7

The defendant is a competent witness.8 The court can require the plaintiff and her child to submit to a blood test in order to determine the parentage of the child.9 Blood group tests can determine the non-paternity of a child and the results of such tests are admissible in evidence, 10 but are not considered as conclusive.11

A child conceived during the existence of a lawful marital relation is presumed in law to be legitimate-a procreation of the husband and wife. Such presumption is not conclusive and may be rebutted by evidence, which must be clear and convincing, that there was no sexual connection between husband and wife during the time in which the child must have been conceived.12 (c) Charge to Jury. This is a case brought under Section 12110 and several subsequent sections of the General Code, which provide in substance that when an unmarried woman, who has been delivered, or is pregnant with a bastard child, makes a complaint in writing before the proper court, the person charged with being the father of said child shall be arrested and brought before 1.

the court to answer such complaint. If no compromise can be effected, the law provides that the issue shall be tried before a jury. It, therefore, now becomes your duty to determine in accordance with these instructions, whether or not.... the defendant in this case, is the father of the child of which was born on or about ... ..

A proceeding in bastardy, although somewhat criminal in form, or what is sometimes called quasi-criminal, is clearly of a civil nature, and the relief sought and the end to be attained are of a civil character. You are the sole judges of the facts in this case and of the weight and credibility to be given to the testimony of the different witnesses. The court is the sole judge of the law, but it is the duty of the jury to find and determine the facts and apply thereto the law as given by the court.

The complaint is no evidence of guilt and before the defendant can be found guilty, as charged in the complaint filed herein, the plaintiff must prove that the defendant had intercourse with the plaintiff within the period of gestation, before the birth of the child, and the other allegations contained in the complaint by a preponderance of the evidence.

A preponderance of the evidence means the greater weight of evidence; but this is not determined solely by the greater number of witnesses testifying in relation to any particular fact or state of facts. It means that the testimony on the part of the party on whom the burden rests must have greater weight in your estimation; have a more convincing effect than that opposed to it. If you should find the evidence to be evenly balanced, it would be your duty to bring in a verdict for the defendant. 

Circumstantial Evidence 

While the plaintiff must prove her case by a preponderance of evidence, still such proof need not be entirely direct evidence of persons who saw the occurrence sought to be proved, but facts may also be proved by circumstantial evidence, that is, by proof of circumstances, if any, such as give rise to a reasonable inference in the minds of the jury of the truth of the facts alleged and sought to be proved, provided such circumstances, together with all the evidence in the case, constitute a preponderance of the evidence.

Credibility 

In determining the credit you will give a witness and the weight and value you will attach to his testimony you should consider his conduct and appearance before you, his interest, if any, in the result of the case; his motives; his bias or prejudice, if any has been disclosed; the opportunity the witness had to be informed or to ith 337

BASTARDY 

observe as to the matters testified to by him; his intelligence; his inclination and disposition to tell the truth, or otherwise, and the probability or improbability of his statements taken together in connection with the other facts and circumstances shown in evidence. Taking all these matters into account, it is your duty to give to each of the witnesses such credit, and to the testimony of each witness such value, as you may think proper.

Impeachment 

Evidence has been introduced here for the purpose of impeaching a witness. A witness is successfully impeached, when the evidence satisfies the jury that the witness is not worthy of credit. Whether a witness has been impeached is a matter for the jury. If the jury believes the evidence offered for the purpose of impeaching a witness, shows that a witness is not worthy of belief, in the opinion of the jury, then the jury would set aside his testimony and would not consider it further, unless it was corroborated by other evidence or circumstances in the case. After all the credibility of a witness is a matter peculiarly for the jury even though evidence may be introduced for the purpose of impeaching a witness. 

Conflicting Evidence 

If there is a real or apparent conflict in the evidence, it is your duty to reconcile that conflict so far as possible. 

It is your duty to determine what you will accept as true, and what you will reject as false.

Avoid Speculations 

You should determine this case according to the evidence produced and submitted to you in open court, in accordance with these instructions, and upon nothing else. 

You will not indulge in any speculation or suppositions concerning what any witness or witnesses might have testified to, had they been permitted to testify, when they were not, but you will consider the testimony as it is, and only as it is, in the record. 

Witness in Own Behalf

The defendant is a competent witness in his own behalf, and his testimony is to be received by you and weighed by the same rules as the testimony of other witnesses. In passing upon what weight you will give to his testimony, you may take into consideration the fact that he is the defendant, and his interest in the result of the trial.

Time of Pregnancy 

The plaintiff alleges that she became pregnant by the defendant on. 

It is not necessary that she prove the exact time. If you are satisfied by a preponderance of the evidence, within the limitations of 338

these instructions, that she became pregnant within the period of gestation, by the defendant, then it is your duty to bring in a verdict against the defendant. 

Exhibition of Child 

The court permitted the plaintiff to exhibit the child to the jury. This was permitted to enable you to judge whether or not it shows a resemblance to the defendant, and this is a circumstance which you can consider in determining the question as to whether or not the defendant is the father of the child. 

Intercourse with Others on If you find from a preponderance of all the evidence that the plaintiff had been delivered of a bastard child and that the defendant is the father of such bastard child, it would make no difference how immoral the plaintiff has been, or what acts of intercourse she has had with other men, or that she gave birth to another illegitimate child, as the purpose of this proceeding is to determine the paternity of such bastard child born on

If you should find that the plaintiff had intercourse with others, about the time the child in question was begotten, it does not necessarily follow that your finding should be for the defendant, but it is a circumstance you should consider in determining the questions as to whether or not the defendant is the father of the child.

Void Marriage one Evidence has been introduced tending to show that the plaintiff was a party to a pretended marriage with 

You are instructed that marriage of the plaintiff is no defense, if the marriage was void, but the facts connected with such pretended marriage are circumstances you should consider in determining whether or not the defendant is the father of the child. 

Declarations 

If you believe from the evidence that the plaintiff, at the time of her travail and while giving birth to the child mentioned in the evidence, declared that the defendant was the father of the child, such declaration may be considered in connection with all the other testimony in the case and you may give it such weight as you may deem proper. (Johnson vs. Walker, 39 So. 49.)

Arguments of Counsel 

You must decide this case upon the testimony which has been given by the witnesses. Statements made by counsel in arguments to the court upon the evidence or arguments to you, as to what facts are shown by the evidence, are not to be considered as evidence by you, unless 339

BASTARDY 

it corresponds with your memory of the evidence as you heard the witnesses give it.

Credibility of Plaintiff 

The plaintiff is a proper witness in her own behalf, and the question of her credibility is left to the jury. It is your duty to take into consideration any variations in her testimony at the preliminary examination, which has been introduced in evidence in this case, and her testimony before the jury, if any there be, and also any other confessions or statements which she may have made at any time, if the evidence discloses any such, which does not agree with her testimony.

One form of verdict will be given you, when you retire to the jury room. If you should find for the plaintiff, you should bring in a verdict of guilty; if you find for the defendant, not guilty. As has been stated to you before, in these instructions, this is a civil case and only threefourth of your number need agree before you can bring in a verdict. 

When you retire, choose one of your number foreman, and when you have arrived at a verdict, those of you who concur therein, should sign your names thereto, and your foreman will deliver the same to the court in a sealed envelope. (d) Verdict. Three-fourths or more of the jury can render a verdict.1 

For challenges, see Secs. 11419-52 and 11419-53 G. C.

SEC. 12122-1 G. C. PATERNITY TESTS IN ILLEGITI- MACY PROCEEDINGS. 

Whenever it shall be relevant to the defense in a bastardy proceeding, the trial court on motion of the defendant, shall order that the complainant, her child, and the defendant submit to one or more blood-grouping tests to determine whether or not the defendant can be excluded as being the father of the child. The tests shall be made by duly qualified physicians or other qualified persons, not to exceed three, to be selected by the court, and under such restrictions and directions as the court or judge shall deem proper. In cases where exclusion is established the results of the tests together with the finding of the expert or experts of the fact of non-paternity shall be receivable in evidence. Such experts shall be subject to cross-examination by both parties after the court has caused them to disclose their findings to the court or to the court and jury. Whenever the court orders such blood-grouping tests to be taken and one of the parties shall refuse to submit to such test, such fact shall be disclosed upon the trial unless good cause is shown to the contrary. In the event such tests have been made prior to the trial, the results thereof shall be receivable in evidence. The court shall determine how and by whom the costs of such examination shall be paid.2

DISCUSSION: 

On the trial of a bastardy proceeding instituted by an unmarried woman whose child was conceived during the existence of a prior marital relation, plaintiff, to establish the nonpaternity of her former husband, may call as a witness a qualified expert who may be permitted to testify that the findings and result of a standard and recognized blood-grouping test made by him from the blood of plaintiff, the child, and her former husband, excluded the former husband as the father of the child.

The findings and result of such blood-grouping test admitted in evidence are not conclusive of nonpaternity, but may be considered for whatever weight they may have in proving the fact.3 

2. Sec. 12122-1 G. C., 118 O. L., 570, effective Sept. 2, 1939. 3. 

State vs. Clark, 144 O. S. 305, 58 N. E. (2d) 773; Slovak vs. Holod, 63 Ohio App., 16, 24 N. E. (2d) 962. 342

BLOOD-GROUPING TESTS. 

Whenever it shall be relevant in a civil or criminal action or proceeding to determine the paternity or identity of any person, the trial court on motion, shall order any party to the action and any person involved in the controversy or proceeding to submit to one or more blood-grouping tests, to be made by duly qualified physicians or other qualified persons not to exceed three, to be selected by the court and under such restrictions or directions as the court or judge shall deem proper. In cases where exclusion is established the results of the tests together with the findings of the expert or experts of the fact of non-paternity shall be receivable in evidence. Such experts shall be subject to cross-examination by both parties after the court has caused them to disclose their findings to the court or to the court and jury. Whenever the court orders such blood-grouping tests to be taken and one of the parties shall refuse to submit to such test, such fact shall be disclosed upon the trial unless good cause is shown to the contrary. The court shall determine how and by whom the costs of such examination shall be paid.4 

DISCUSSION: 

This section has no application in a case asking for modification of non-support order where the defendant had been found guilty in a previous bastardy action.1 

SEC. 12123 G. C. ORDER OF COURT WHEN ACCUSED ADJUDGED REPUTED FATHER. 

If, in person or by counsel, the accused confesses in court that the accusation is true or, if the jury finds him guilty, he shall be adjudged the reputed father of the illegitimate child if said child is alive, and the court shall thereupon adjudge that he pay to the complainant such sum as the court may find to be necessary for her support, maintenance and necessary expenses, caused by pregnancy and childbirth together with costs of prosecution, and a reasonable weekly sum to be paid complainant for support and maintenance of said child up to eighteen years of age. In the event said child is not born alive, or is not living at the time of said plea or finding of guilty, the court shall order the accused to pay the complainant such sum as the court may find to be necessary for her support, maintenance and necessary expense caused by pregnancy, including therein a reasonable amount for maintenance of said child until its death, and its funeral expenses. 

The court shall require the reputed father to give security for the payment of support, maintenance and necessary expenses of the complainant caused by the pregnancy and childbirth together with the cost of prosecution. If he neglects or refuses to give it, and pay the cost of prosecution, he shall be 4. 

Sec. 12122-2 G. C., 118 O. L., 571, effective Sept. 2, 1939. 

1. State vs. Mercer, 21 O. O., 317, 34 O. L. A., 362, 6 Ohio Sup. 303. 343

BASTARDY 

committed to the jail of the county, there to remain, except as provided in the next following section, until he complies with the order of the court. On the judgment of the juvenile court, execution may issue to the sheriff as in cases of judgments for money in the court of common pleas. Provided, however, that nothing in this section shall be construed as a bar to the prosecution of the accused for failure to support his illegitimate child or children under the provisions of any statute providing for prosecution and punishment for nonsupport of legitimate or illegitimate children; provided further that in any such prosecution the adjudication that accused is the reputed father of the illegitimate child or children shall be admissible in evidence; and provided further that the acquittal of accused in a bastardy proceeding shall be a bar to any such prosecution.2

DISCUSSION: 

The jury should find the defendant guilty or not guilty and if guilty, the court adjudges him the reputed father.3 

The court must also fix the sum necessary for the support of the mother and her necessary expenses during pregnancy. The court and not the jury should hear the evidence on this question.4 Attorney fees or expenses. for care of the child is no part of the judgment,5 nor can a civil action for support of the child be maintained against the father.6 3. 4.

A judgment requiring father to pay for support of mother during pregnancy and expenses of childbirth, or stand committed to jail is not unconstitutional.7 

The order to give a bond for security to perform the order of the court is a part of the final judgment.8 

This section relating to the duties and obligations of an adjudged reputed father to his illegitimate child, is in derogation of the common law and must be strictly construed, and, there being no mention in this section of the reputed father's responsibility for the expense incurred in the support of the child from birth to the adjudication establishing the child's paternity, it is not error for a trial court to refuse to allow any amount for the support of the child during such period,9 but the father can be compelled to pay for the child's support from its birth to the time of the adjudication.10 5. 6. 7. 8. 9. 10.

Harris vs. Jones, 34 O. O., 489, 71 N. E. (2d) 491. 344

It is not error for the trial court to enter judgment on the verdict of a jury at once, and within the period for filing a motion for new trial. The obligation of the bond is confined to payment of support, maintenance, and necessary expenses of the complainant caused by the pregnancy and childbirth together with the costs of prosecution, and does not run to any reasonable sum fixed by the court for support and maintenance of the child until it becomes 18 years of age.11

SEC. 12124 G. C. LAW RELATING TO INSOLVENT DEBTORS. 

After having been confined in prison for three months, for failing to comply with the order provided for in the next preceding section, such putative father shall be entitled to the benefits of the law relating to insolvent debtors, in like manner as persons imprisoned for debt. But before he shall be entitled thereto, he must give at least three days' notice to the complainant or her attorney of his intention to apply therefor.12 

12. Sec. 12124, G. C., R. S. 5627. 346

SEC. 12125 G. C. DEATH OF MOTHER SHALL NOT ABATE PROSECUTION. 

The death of the mother shall not abate a prosecution instituted by her. A suggestion of the fact shall be made, and the name of her executor or administrator substituted on the record for that of the mother. Such executor or administrator shall not be liable for costs. In such case, the testimony of the mother, reduced to writing before the justice, may be read in evidence.1

SEC. 12128 G. C. JUSTICE TO FURNISH TRANSCRIPT ON FAILURE OF OFFICER TO ARREST ACCUSED. 

When, from the return of the officer on the warrant, it appears that the accused could not be arrested, upon demand, the justice forthwith shall make a certified transcript of the proceedings before him, including copies of the complaint and warrant, with the return thereon, and deliver them to the complainant, or her agent or attorney.2

SEC. 12129 G. C. 

ORDER OF ATTACHMENT AND GROUNDS THEREFOR. 

Upon filing such transcript in the office of the clerk of the common pleas court in the county in which the justice resides, such clerk shall issue an order of attachment when there is filed in his office an affidavit of the complainant, her agent or attorney, showing: 

1. That she is the mother of a bastard child, or pregnant with a child which, if born alive, will be a bastard; 

2. That the accused person is the father of such child; 

3. The existence of one or more of the following grounds: 

That the accused is not a resident of this state; or, has absconded with the intent to defraud complainant; or, has left the county of his residence to avoid the service of a warrant; or, so conceals himself, that a warrant cannot be served upon him.3

SEC. 12130 G. C. PROCEEDINGS UNDER ATTACHMENT. 

The order of attachment shall issue without a bond. The amount of property seized thereon shall not exceed one thousand dollars in appraised value. Attachments under this chapter are subject to the provisions of law as to attachments in civil actions, and shall be governed thereby.4 

SEC. 12131 G. C. SERVICE BY PUBLICATION. 

Upon the return of the order of attachment, service may 

1. Sec. 12125, G. C., 110 O. L., 299. 

2. Sec. 12128, G. C., R. S. 5631. 

3. Sec. 12129, G. C., R. S. 5632. 

4. Sec. 12130, G. C., R. S. 5633. 347

BASTARDY 

be had by the publication, for six consecutive weeks, in a newspaper of general circulation in the county wherein the cause is pending, of a notice of the pendency of the proceeding, stating its object, the substance of the complaint, and that an order of attachment has been issued and served therein. In such case, copies of the complaint and order of attachment, with the return thereon forthwith must be deposited in the postoffice, directed to the accused at his place of residence, unless it appears to the court, by affidavit, or otherwise, that such residence is unknown to the complainant, and could not, with reasonable diligence be ascertained by her.5

SEC.12132 G. C. PERSONAL SERVICE OF COPIES OF COMPLAINT. 

If the defendant's place of residence is known, personal service of certified copies of the complaint and order of attachment, with the returns thereon, may be made at complainant's election, instead of service by publication. The cause may be heard and determined after the expiration of six weeks from the time of personal service, or the first publication of the notice provided for in the next preceding section.6

SEC. 12133 G. C. ORDER OF COURT WITH RESPECT TO ATTACHED PROPERTY. 

If, on such trial, the accused be adjudged to be the reputed father of the child, the court shall order that unless, within a day to be fixed by it, he pays the sum adjudged against him, with costs of prosecution, so much of the property remaining in the hands of the officer, after applying money from the sale of perishable property, and so much of the personal property, lands and tenements, if any, as are necessary to satisfy such order, be sold, under the same restrictions and regulations as if levied on by execution. The money arising therefrom, with any amount recovered from the garnishee, shall be subject to the order and control of the court, and be applied to satisfy such order in such sums and at such times as the court orders and directs. If there be not enough to satisfy the order, it shall stand, and execution may issue thereon for the residue, as in judgment at law. Any surplus of attached property, or its proceeds, shall be returned to the defendant.7

SEC. 12134 G. C. PROSECUTION BY OTHERS IN CASE OF DISABILITY OF MOTHER. 

In case of the death or disability of an unmarried woman who would otherwise be eligible to make complaint as pro-
